# The Kervaire invariant problem at the prime three

Coordinate `EN-104-000-0000-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M3-C-TO-H-0630` | `PM-309.1` | status ACTIVE | original slug `The-Kervaire-Invariant-Problem-at-the-Prime-Three-September-24-2026` | date 2026-09-24 | derived from the openai/math preprint The-Kervaire-Invariant-Problem-at-the-Prime-Three-September-24-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We solve the Kervaire invariant problem at the prime three for the standard Kervaire classes in the mod-three Adams spectral sequence. These classes survive precisely at indices 0, 2, and 3, and each surviving detection coset contains an element of additive order three. In particular, there is an order-three Kervaire element in stem 322.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We solve the Kervaire invariant problem at the prime three for the standard Kervaire classes in the mod-three Adams spectral sequence. These classes survive precisely at indices 0, 2, and 3, and each surviving detection coset contains an element of additive order three. In particular, there is an order-three Kervaire element in stem 322.

- **claim-kervaire-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-classification)): At the prime three, $$K=K_3=\{0,2,3\}.$$ Thus the surviving classes occur in stems $10$, $106$, and $322$, and each admits an additive-order-three representative. The class $b_1$ and every $b_j$ with $j\geq4$ fail to survive.
  - Explanation: At the prime three the surviving standard Kervaire classes and those with an order-three representative coincide, and the index set is exactly the one listed in the theorem.
- **claim-unstable-consequences** (classification, [source](#stmt-intro-unstable-consequences)): 1. *There are homotopy equivalences of H-spaces $$\Omega S^{163}\{3\}\simeq T^{163}(3)\times\Omega T^{487}(3), \qquad BW_{81}\simeq\Omega T^{487}(3).$$ Consequently, $W_{81}\simeq\Omega^2T^{487}(3)$.* 2. *For every integer $n>1$, the underlying space $\Omega S^{2n+1}\{3\}$ has a nontrivial product decomposition if and only if $n\in\{3,27,81\}$. For every other $n>1$ it is mod-three atomic, hence indecomposable. Here a product is nontrivial when both factors are noncontractible.* 3. *For every integer $n>1$, the homotopy type $T^{2n+1}(3)$ admits a homotopy-associative H-space multiplication if and only if $n\in\{3,27,81\}$. The multiplication on $T^{163}(3)$ supplied by the displayed product is homotopy associative and homotopy commutative. For this multiplication, the third-power map on $T^{163}(3)$ is null-homotopic, as is the third-power map on the loop H-space $\Omega T^{487}(3)$.
  - Explanation: The classification yields product decompositions and H-space structures on Anick spaces and looped sphere fibres exactly at the indices where the strong Kervaire condition holds, and atomicity elsewhere.
- **claim-classical-endpoints** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-classical)): One has $0,2\in K_3$ and $1\notin K$.
  - Explanation: The classical endpoint indices survive with order-three representatives while the middle index supports a nonzero Adams–Novikov differential.
- **claim-filtration-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-filtration-comparison)): There are augmented comparisons from the ordinary Adams–Novikov spectral sequence to the mod-$3$ Adams spectral sequence and to the homotopy fixed point spectral sequences for $E^{hG}$ and $E^{hH}$. If a sphere class $\alpha$ is detected by $b_j$, then $\alpha$ has Adams–Novikov filtration exactly two. It admits a permanent representative $$x\in\mathcal N_2^{2,\,4\cdot3^{j+1}}, \qquad \Phi(x)\in\mathbf F_3^\times b_j .$$
  - Explanation: Augmented comparisons relate the Adams–Novikov tower to the mod-three Adams and homotopy fixed point spectral sequences, and a sphere class detected by b_j has Adams–Novikov filtration exactly two.
- **claim-two-line-basis** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-two-line-basis)): Fix $j\geq0$. For $$n\in\{j,j-2,\ldots,j\bmod2\},\qquad d=3^n,\qquad L=\frac{3^{j+1-n}+1}{4},$$ the classes $$\label{det:uncorrected-beta} \beta'_{Ld/d}=\delta_3\delta_{v_1^d}(v_2^{Ld})$$ form an $\mathbf F_3$-basis of $\mathcal N_2^{2,\,4\cdot3^{j+1}}$. Every member with $L>1$ has zero Thom image.
  - Explanation: An explicit family of uncorrected beta classes forms a basis of the two-line in the detector degree, and every member with parameter above one has zero Thom image.
- **claim-cm-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-cm-evaluation)): For $j\geq2$, every $$x\in\mathcal N_2^{2,\,4\cdot3^{j+1}} \quad\text{with}\quad \Phi(x)\in\mathbf F_3^\times b_j$$ has nonzero image in $H^2(G;E_{4\cdot3^{j+1}})$.
  - Explanation: Any two-line class whose Thom image is a nonzero multiple of b_j evaluates to a nonzero class in the integral cohomology of the cyclic group.
- **claim-two-layer-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-two-layer)): Let a finite group $J$ act on an even spectrum $Z$, and put $Y=Z^{hJ}$. Let $F^s=\operatorname{fib}(Y\to\mathop{\mathrm{Tot}}_{s-1}C^\bullet)$ be the decreasing tower of its group-cochain resolution. If a class in $\pi_{T-2}F^2$, with $T$ even, has nonzero symbol in $H^2(J;\pi_T Z)$, its image in $\pi_{T-2}Y$ is nonzero.
  - Explanation: A nonzero filtration-two symbol in group cohomology forces the corresponding homotopy class in the fixed point spectrum to be nonzero, using only a finite two-layer argument.
- **claim-actual-detector** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-actual-detector)): Let $j\geq2$, and let $\alpha\in\pi_{4\cdot3^{j+1}-2}S_{(3)}$ be detected by $b_j$ in the mod-$3$ Adams spectral sequence. Its image under the unit $$\pi_{4\cdot3^{j+1}-2}S_{(3)} \longrightarrow \pi_{4\cdot3^{j+1}-2}(E^{hG})$$ is nonzero and has finite additive order. Its filtration-two symbol is the nonzero image of the Adams–Novikov representative in Lemma <a href="#det:filtration-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="det:filtration-comparison">4</a>.
  - Explanation: An actual sphere class detected by b_j with j at least two maps to a nonzero class of finite additive order in the homotopy of the fixed point spectrum.
- **claim-semilinear-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-semilinear-trace)): Let a finite group $J$ act on a characteristic-zero domain $A$ and semilinearly on a free rank-one module $Ae$. Suppose the kernel of $J\to\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(A)$ acts trivially on $Ae$. Then the additive trace $$\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_J:Ae\longrightarrow (Ae)^J,\qquad z\longmapsto\sum_{\gamma\in J}\gamma z$$ is nonzero.
  - Explanation: The additive trace on a free rank-one semilinear module over a characteristic-zero domain is nonzero when the kernel of the action on the ring acts trivially on the module.
- **claim-homotopy-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-homotopy-transfer)): Let a finite group $J$ act coherently on a spectrum $Z$. For every integer $d$, the additive trace on $\pi_d Z$ factors as $$\pi_d Z\longrightarrow \pi_d(Z^{hJ}) \longrightarrow \pi_d Z .$$
  - Explanation: The additive trace on homotopy groups factors through the homotopy fixed point spectrum via the homotopy transfer.
- **claim-full-gap-failure** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-det-full-gap)): For every $n\in\mathbf Z$, the group $\pi_{-2n}(E^{hG})$ contains an element of infinite additive order. In particular, $\pi_{-2}(E^{hG})\ne0$.
  - Explanation: Infinite-order classes exist in every even degree of the fixed point homotopy, so the proposed vanishing in degree minus two is false.
- **claim-conditional-repair** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-det-conditional-repair)): Suppose that the graded groups $\pi_*(E^{hG})$ are $972$-periodic and that $\pi_{-2}(E^{hG})$ is torsion-free. Then $b_j$ does not survive for any $j\geq4$.
  - Explanation: Under periodicity and torsion-freeness in degree minus two, the higher detectors could not survive; these hypotheses are not assumed later in the paper.
- **claim-finite-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-det-finite-boundary)): Let $F^{s+1}\to F^s$, $s\geq0$, be a decreasing tower of spectra, or of genuine spectra for a finite group. If $\alpha\in\pi_VF^s$ maps to zero in $\pi_VF^0$, its layer symbol is zero by $E_{s+1}$. If that symbol is initially nonzero, its first disappearance is an incoming differential of length at most $s$.
  - Explanation: A class that maps to zero in filtration zero has its layer symbol killed by an incoming differential of bounded length.
- **bound-euler-powers** (bound, [source](#stmt-det-euler-bound)): In the cofree genuine spectra associated to $E$, the actual faithful Euler classes satisfy $$a_{\lambda\|H}^{79}=0,\qquad a_\lambda^{235}=0.$$ Consequently, the $H$-Tate pages kill the unit by a differential of length at most $157$, and the $G$-Tate pages kill it by a differential of length at most $469$.
  - Explanation: Finite powers of the faithful Euler classes vanish in the cofree genuine spectra, so the unit is killed on Tate pages by a differential of bounded length.
- **claim-tangent-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-tangent)): For $1\le i\le5$, the operator $D_g$ sends $P_i$ into the span of $P_0,\ldots,P_{i-1}$, with a nonzero coefficient of $P_{i-1}$. The operator $D_\sigma$ lowers the index by at least three, and its coefficient at $P_{i-3}$, for $i\ge3$, is $a^{3^{i-3}}$, where $$a\,a^{27}=-1.$$ Moreover, $D_g u/u$ has a nonzero $P_5$-term and $D_\sigma u/u$ has leading term $a^{27}P_3$. One may choose a weight-one period $u$ and linear coordinates $$y_0=[D_\sigma u/u],\qquad y_1=P_4,\qquad y_2=P_5,\qquad l_i=D_\sigma y_i$$ such that $$l_0=-P_0,\qquad \operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak m}E_* =k[u^{\pm1},y_0,l_0,y_1,l_1,y_2,l_2], \qquad \sigma y_i=y_i+l_i,\quad \sigma l_i=l_i,\quad \sigma u=u.$$ The weights of $y_0,y_1,y_2$ are $0,2,8$, respectively.
  - Explanation: The generator acts triangularly on the tangent coordinates, giving independent linear coordinates for the coefficient calculation.
- **claim-first-coefficient-page** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-graded-tate)): There is an isomorphism of trigraded algebras $$\label{coef:first-page} \mathcal E_1 =k[b^{\pm1},u^{\pm1},N_0,N_1,N_2] \otimes\Lambda(e,z_0,z_1,z_2).$$ Here $e,z_i$ have cohomological degree one and internal degree zero. Their cyclic cocycle values at $\sigma$ are $1,y_i$, respectively. Their $\mathfrak m$-filtrations are $0,1$, and $N_i$ has filtration three. The classes $b^{\pm1}$, $N_i$, and the class denoted $u^3$ lift to cycles of the complete coefficient complex.
  - Explanation: The first page of the adic spectral sequence on the Tate complex is a tensor product of a polynomial algebra and an exterior algebra.
- **claim-integral-lift** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-integral-lift)): After compatible nonzero scalar normalizations, the auxiliary coefficient spectral sequence has $$d_1(u)=uz_0,\qquad d_1(e)=ez_0,\qquad d_1(z_i)=z_i z_0\ (i=1,2),\qquad d_1(z_0)=0.$$ Consequently $$\label{coef:second-page} \mathcal E_2 =k[b^{\pm1},u^{\pm3},N_0,N_1,N_2] \otimes\Lambda(eu,z_1u,z_2u,z_0).$$ Its only nonzero differential on these generators is $$d_2(z_0)=c\,bN_0,\qquad c\in k^\times.$$ There are no further differentials. In particular the leading cohomology class $eu$ is represented by an actual element $x_0\in E_{-2}$ satisfying $$x_0/u\equiv1\pmod{\mathfrak m},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_H(x_0)=0.$$ The element $x_0$ may be chosen of $T$-weight one.
  - Explanation: Only one differential survives on the generators, and the leading class lifts to an actual trace-zero period.
- **claim-cyclic-coefficient-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-coef-model)): Set $x_i=g^i x_0$, with indices read modulo nine. Then $$\label{coef:ring-model} \left( \frac{W(k)[x_0,\ldots,x_8,(x_0\cdots x_8)^{-1}]} {(x_i+x_{i+3}+x_{i+6}:0\le i<3)} \right)^{\wedge}_{(3,x_i/x_0-1)} \xrightarrow{\ \cong\ } E_*$$ is an isomorphism of $W(k)$-linear graded $G$-rings, with $\|x_i\|=-2$ and $g(x_i)=x_{i+1}$.
  - Explanation: The completed coefficient ring is presented as a quotient of a polynomial ring in orbit generators, equivariantly for the cyclic group.
- **claim-h-cohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-h-cohomology)): Put $q_i=\mathop{\mathrm{N}}_H(x_i)$, $q=q_0$. There are norm parameters $n_1,n_2$ such that, with $K=k[[n_1,n_2]]$, $$\label{coef:h-tate-ring} \widehat H^*(H;E_*)= K[q^{\pm1},b^{\pm1}]\otimes_K\Lambda_K(V), \qquad \|q\|=(0,-6),\quad \|b\|=(2,0),\quad \|V\|=(1,4).$$ The module $V$ is free of rank three, with cyclic-cocycle basis $$a_i=x_i/q_i,\qquad 0\le i<3.$$ The group $g$ cyclically permutes this basis semilinearly over $K$. Equivalently, $q_i/q-1$, $i=1,2$, are regular parameters of $K$. For $\mathfrak n=(n_1,n_2)$, the weights on $\mathfrak n/\mathfrak n^2$ are $6,-2$, and $V/\mathfrak n V$ is a single three-term Jordan block for $g$.
  - Explanation: The Tate cohomology for the subgroup is a Laurent algebra tensored with an exterior algebra on a free module of rank three, permuted cyclically.
- **claim-scalar-cycles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-scalar-cycles)): On every nonzero topological Tate page, $$A=qb^{-2}$$ is a permanent-cycle unit, and every element of $K$ is a cycle.
  - Explanation: The scalar unit and all coefficient elements are cycles on every nonzero topological Tate page.
- **claim-endomorphism-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-rigidity)): Let $B$ be an Artinian local ring with residue characteristic three, and let $F_1,F_2$ be deformations over $B$ of formal groups of finite height. A homomorphism $F_1\to F_2$ is determined by its reduction. The same assertion holds over a complete local base by passage to its Artinian quotients.
  - Explanation: A homomorphism between deformations of finite-height formal groups over an Artinian base is determined by its reduction.
- **claim-fixed-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-fixed-geometry)): The eigenvalue condition $$\sigma x_i=\zeta x_i$$ defines a framed strict formal $\mathcal O$-module deformation space of relative height three. Its ungraded base and Lie period are $$D_0=W(k)[\zeta][[r_1,r_2]],\qquad r_i=x_i/x_0-1\ (i=1,2),\qquad w=x_0.$$ The full ungraded $H$-fixed base is equivalently $$\label{coef:nonreduced-fixed} W(k)[[s,r_1,r_2]]/(s^2+3s+3),\qquad 1+s=\sigma x_0/x_0.$$ In particular its characteristic-three fibre retains the relation $s^2=0$.* *Set $\bar D=D_0/(\pi)$. There is a $\mathcal C$-invariant Hasse section $h_1$ in the line $w^{-2}$, given by the first strict coefficient of $\sigma$. On its zero divisor there is a $\mathcal C$-invariant Hasse section $h$ in $w^{-8}$, given by the second strict coefficient. The ring $$D'=\bar D/(h_1)$$ is a formal disc with parameter $hw^8$; here ideals generated by line sections are read after a period trivialization.* *Evaluation in cyclic cohomology and reduction modulo $\pi$ identify $$K\cong\bar D^{\,3},\qquad q\longmapsto w^3.$$ The evaluated $V$ is the $\bar D^3$-span of $1,x_1/x_0,x_2/x_0$ in the line $w^{-2}$. There is a $\mathcal C$-fixed primitive element $\theta\in V$, the restriction of the class of $t_1$, which evaluates to $h_1$ up to a nonzero scalar.* *One may choose a $T$-basis $v,\theta,y$ of $V$ with weights $-2,0,6$, with $v=x_0/q$. Modulo $\mathfrak n$, $$\label{coef:jordan} g\theta=\theta,\qquad gy=y+c_1\theta,\qquad gv=v+c_2y+c_3\theta,\qquad c_1c_2\ne0.$$ Using evaluation to identify cube sections with their unique preimages, put $$J=h_1^3\in Kq^{-2},\qquad F=q^2J,\qquad S_0=K/(F)\cong(D')^3,\qquad W=(V/K\theta)\otimes_K S_0.$$ Then $F$ is a regular parameter of $K$, and there is an injective $\mathcal C$-equivariant evaluation $$W\longrightarrow w^{-2}\quad\text{over }D'.$$ After trivializing the line, the evaluations of $v,y$ have orders zero and one, respectively.
  - Explanation: The eigenvalue condition defines a strict formal module deformation space whose Hasse sections give a formal disc and a Jordan structure on the exterior module.
- **claim-infinite-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-infinite-action)): Every open subgroup of $\mathcal C$ has infinite image on $D'$. Its invariant Laurent functions are constants. These assertions remain valid after a finite extension of the residue field, with the group acting trivially on the extended constants.
  - Explanation: Open subgroups of the centralizer act with infinite image on the curve, so invariant Laurent functions are constants.
- **claim-hasse-powers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-hasse-powers)): A nonzero invariant meromorphic section of $w^i$ is a constant multiple of $h^d$, where $8d=-i$ and $d\in\mathbf Z$. If it is integral, then $d\ge0$. There is no nonzero invariant meromorphic section of $w^i\chi$ for any nontrivial constant sign character $\chi:\mathcal C\to\{\pm1\}$.
  - Explanation: Invariant meromorphic sections of line powers are constant multiples of integer powers of the Hasse section, with no sign-twisted ones.
- **claim-determinant-line** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-determinant)): After a finite extension of constants there is an equivariant isomorphism $$\label{coef:determinant-formula} \det(D'\otimes_{S_0}W)\cong w^{-9}\chi,$$ where $\chi:\mathcal C\to\{\pm1\}$ is a sign character whose restriction to $T$ has weight $13$, and is therefore nontrivial.
  - Explanation: The determinant line is a power of the Lie line twisted by a nontrivial sign character, via crystalline Dieudonné theory.
- **claim-hom-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-coef-hom-vanishing)): For every integer $n$, including after meromorphic localization, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal C,S_0}(W,P_n)=0.$$ Every nonzero invariant integral vector in $WP_n$ evaluates as a constant multiple of $h^d$, where $$8d=2-3n,\qquad d\ge0.$$ If $h$ occurs in $WP_{-2}$, the corresponding line $$\ell=hP_2\subset W$$ is primitive and stable. After extension to $D'$, its quotient has character $w^{-15}\chi$. More generally, if $h^{1+3k}$ occurs in $WP_{-2-8k}$, $k\ge0$, then $h$ already occurs in $WP_{-2}$.
  - Explanation: No equivariant homomorphisms exist from the plane module to the scalar lines, and invariant integral vectors evaluate to Hasse powers.
- **claim-greek-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-greek-evaluation)): With compatible nonzero scalar normalizations, the images of the indicated Greek-letter classes are $$\alpha_1=\theta,\qquad \beta_1=bJ,\qquad \beta_{3/3}=bJ^3,\qquad \beta_4=\pm bJ^5.$$ In particular, $$\label{h:d5-b} d_5(b)=\theta b^3,\qquad d_5(\theta)=0.$$
  - Explanation: The Greek-letter Adams–Novikov classes evaluate to explicit generators of the subgroup Tate page, giving the first differential on the periodicity class.
- **claim-normalized-d9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-normalized-d9)): On the cohomology of <a href="#h:sector-complex" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:sector-complex">[h:sector-complex]</a>, the operation $\kappa=A^2d_9$ preserves both the $A$- and $b$-exponents and lowers exterior degree by one. It is a derivation and satisfies $$\label{h:kappa-theta} \kappa(\Theta)=cF,\qquad c\in k^{\times}.$$
  - Explanation: The normalized ninth differential preserves exponents, lowers exterior degree by one and sends the generator to a nonzero multiple of the parameter.
- **claim-mu-and-matrix** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-mu-and-matrix)): One has $\mu\in\mathbf F_3^{\times}$; in integer exponents we use its representative $1$ or $-1$. Moreover, $$\Psi=\mu\Theta\wedge(-)$$ on the complementary two-plane, and $$\begin{aligned} \label{h:e6} E_6(H)&=K[A^{\pm1},B^{\pm1}] \otimes\Lambda\bigl(\Theta,b^{-1-\mu}(V/K\theta)\bigr),\\ \label{h:e10} E_{10}(H)&=S_0[A^{\pm1},B^{\pm1}] \otimes\Lambda\bigl(b^{-1-\mu}W\bigr). \end{aligned}$$ The $d_9$ is the Koszul differential on $\Theta,F$ and is zero on the other displayed generators.
  - Explanation: The scalar governing the fifth differential is a unit of the prime field, and the sixth and tenth pages are described explicitly.
- **claim-exclude-minus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-exclude-minus)): The case $\mu=-1$ is impossible.
  - Explanation: The negative sign case is excluded because the unit could not be hit within the finite Euler bound.
- **claim-d17-d33** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-d17-d33)): One has $\epsilon\ne0$ and $\eta/\epsilon=2$. After rescaling $h$, the $d_{17}$ coefficients in <a href="#h:first-plane-coefficients" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:first-plane-coefficients">[h:first-plane-coefficients]</a> are $p$ and $p-1$. The next nonzero change is $d_{33}$, which contracts the surviving $\ell$-generator by a unit times $Z$. Its cohomology is $$\label{h:e34} E_{34}(H)=k[A^{\pm1},C^{\pm1}]\otimes\Lambda(U), \qquad C=B^3=b^9,\qquad U=BX.$$
  - Explanation: The coefficients of the seventeenth differential are determined and the thirty-third contracts the surviving line generator, leaving an explicit page.
- **claim-unit-death-h** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-unit-death)): The only possible differential between $E_{34}(H)$ and $E_{105}(H)$ is $$\label{h:d53} d_{53}(C)=e_{53}A^{-8}C^2U,\qquad d_{53}(U)=0, \qquad e_{53}\in k.$$ The class $A^{18}C^{-2}U$ survives to $E_{105}(H)$, and $$\label{h:d105} d_{105}(A^{18}C^{-2}U)\in k^{\times}.$$ In particular, $E_{106}(H)=0$.
  - Explanation: Between the stated pages only one differential is possible, and a one-dimensional source then hits the unit, so the later page vanishes.
- **claim-h-differentials** (theorem, [source](#stmt-h-differentials)): The $H$-Tate calculation has the following properties.* 1. *The first differential is $$d_5(b^m q^n\xi) =(m+2n+2j)b^{m+2}q^n\theta\wedge\xi, \qquad \xi\in\Lambda^jV,$$ up to a single nonzero scalar normalization. Its cohomology has generators $A,B=b^3,\Theta=b^{-1}\theta$ and $b^{-2}(V/K\theta)$ as in <a href="#h:e6" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:e6">[h:e6]</a>, with $\mu=1$. For a fixed $c\in k^{\times}$, the next differential is $$d_9(\Theta)=cA^{-2}F=cb^4J.$$ Equivalently, on surviving $\Theta$-containing representatives, $$d_9\bigl([b^m q^n\theta\wedge\xi]\bigr) =c[b^{m+5}q^nJ\xi],$$ where $\xi$ is a complementary exterior monomial. These raw products are interpreted in the $A,B$ normal form: $A^{-2}$ is a surviving unit, while $b^4$ and $J$ need not survive separately. Its cohomology is <a href="#h:e10" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:e10">[h:e10]</a>.* 2. *The $d_{17}$ is wedge multiplication by the invariant Hasse vector $h$, with common unit shifts and coefficients $p$ on exterior degree zero and $p-1$ on exterior degree one. Its survivors have supports $$\begin{array}{c\|c} \text{component}&p\pmod3\\\hline \text{scalars}&0\\ \ell&2\\ W/\ell&1\\ \det W&0. \end{array}$$* 3. *The nonzero $d_{33}$ contracts $\ell$ to scalars and $\det W$ to $W/\ell$. Under the identifications by $h$ and $h\wedge(-)$, respectively, these maps are multiplication by $h^3$, up to nonzero scalar units and the prescribed degree shifts. Only scalars at $p\equiv0$ and quotient classes at $p\equiv1$ remain, now over $S_0/(Z)=k$.* 4. *The remaining algebra and differentials are <a href="#h:e34" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:e34">[h:e34]</a>, <a href="#h:d53" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:d53">[h:d53]</a>, and <a href="#h:d105" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="h:d105">[h:d105]</a>. The coefficient $e_{53}$ has not been asserted nonzero in this subgroup calculation.
  - Explanation: The subgroup Tate calculation is summarized in four parts covering the early differentials, the Hasse wedge map, the contractions and the final unit-killing differential.
- **claim-tail-exclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-tail-exclusion)): For every $\jmath\geq4$, the class $b_{\jmath}\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_{\mathcal A_3}^{2,4\cdot3^{\jmath+1}} (\mathbf F_3,\mathbf F_3)$ does not survive the classical Adams spectral sequence.
  - Explanation: Every standard Kervaire class with index at least four fails to survive the classical Adams spectral sequence.
- **claim-translation-units** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-translations)): On every nonzero page the class $$\label{g:Delta-unit} \mathcal D=\Delta b^{-6}u'^2$$ is a cycle and a unit. Constants from $W(k)$ act by cycles. There is also a semilinear symmetry commuting with $G$, inducing Witt Frobenius on $k$ and fixing $b$ and $u'$.
  - Explanation: The translation class built from the norm is a cycle and a unit on every nonzero page, and constants act by cycles.
- **claim-mackey** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-mackey)): Suppressing powers of $b$ and the orientation twist $u'$, the scalar and exterior-degree-three groups at $G$ have a common description $\widetilde P_n$. Restriction fits into $$0\longrightarrow \overline k_n\longrightarrow \widetilde P_n\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}P_n^Q \longrightarrow0, \qquad \overline k_n= \begin{cases}k,&3\mid n,\\0,&3\nmid n. \end{cases}$$ Transfer embeds $(P_n)_Q$ in $\widetilde P_n$, with cokernel $\overline k_n$. Moreover $$\label{g:P-tate} \widehat H^d(Q;P_n)=\overline k_n \qquad(d\in\mathbf Z).$$ For $3\mid n$, the exceptional class is represented by $\Delta^{n/3}$; its transfer is the nonzero three-multiple in the restriction kernel. There is a class $c_G$ restricting to $a_0a_1a_2$, and multiplication by $c_G$ identifies the scalar and volume descriptions, including their exceptional kernels.* *In exterior degrees one and two, restriction is injective onto all invariants. These groups are parametrized by transfer of $P_na_0$ and $P_na_0a_1$, respectively.
  - Explanation: The scalar and volume groups at the larger group have a common description with a restriction kernel and Tate cohomology concentrated on one line.
- **claim-early-pages-g** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-early-pages)): At fixed $r_0,n$, the $d_5$ complex in increasing exterior degree is $$\label{g:d5-complex} \widetilde P_n\xrightarrow{\ r_0\operatorname{res}\ }P_n \xrightarrow{\ \pm(r_0+1)D\ }P_n \xrightarrow{\ (r_0+2)\operatorname{tr}\ }\widetilde P_n.$$ Consequently the $E_6$ page has the following rows: $$\label{g:E6} \begin{array}{c\|cccc} r_0&j=0&j=1&j=2&j=3\\\hline 0&\widetilde P_n&P_n^Q&0&\overline k_n\\ 2&\overline k_n&P_n/P_n^Q&DP_n&\overline k_n\\ 1&\overline k_n&0&(P_n)_Q&\widetilde P_n. \end{array}$$ The orientation $u'$ has zero $d_5$ and $d_9$.
  - Explanation: The fifth-differential complex at the larger group is built from restriction, a difference operator and transfer, giving the sixth page rows.
- **claim-e10-description** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-e10-description)): Put $$S_n=P_n/JP_{n+2},\qquad W_n=S_n\otimes\overline M.$$ The main $H$ groups on $E_{10}$ are $S_n,W_n,S_n$ in exterior degrees $0,1,2$, respectively, at $w_0\equiv0\pmod3$. Define transfer modules $$\begin{aligned} \mathcal T_n&= \begin{cases}(S_n)_Q,&n\equiv0,2\pmod3,\\ NS_n,&n\equiv1\pmod3, \end{cases}\label{g:Tn}\\ \mathcal W_n&= \begin{cases}(W_n)_Q,&n\equiv1,2\pmod3,\\ NW_n,&n\equiv0\pmod3. \end{cases}\label{g:Wn} \end{aligned}$$ At $G$ the main groups are:* 1. *in degree zero, a scalar group $\mathcal S_n$ with transfer part $\mathcal T_n$ and one additional restriction generator when $3\mid n$;* 2. *in degree one, the transfer group $\mathcal W_n$;* 3. *in degree two, the transfer group $\mathcal T_n$.* *On these transfer groups restriction is norm and transfer is the displayed projection. The additional groups are one $k$ each, only when $3\mid n$: scalar restriction kernels at $w_0\equiv1,2\pmod3$, and volume groups at every $w_0$. The volume is of $3c_G$ kernel type when $w_0\equiv1$, and of reduced $c_G$ type when $w_0\equiv0,2$.
  - Explanation: The tenth page is described by transfer modules plus one-dimensional restriction kernels in the degrees stated.
- **claim-d13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-d13)): Put $T_0=b^5c_G$ on $E_{13}$. After a nonzero normalization, $$d_{13}B/B=\varepsilon T_0,\qquad d_{13}u'/u'=0,\qquad \varepsilon\ne0.$$ There is a residue $\rho\in\{2,5,8\}\pmod9$ such that the extra scalar families surviving $d_{13}$ occur in the two families $w_0\equiv1\pmod3$ or $w_0\equiv\rho\pmod9$. The extra volume families occur in the three residue families $$w_0\equiv0\pmod3,\qquad 2\pmod9, \qquad \rho+2\pmod9.$$ Scalar generators outside the transfer part survive precisely at $w_0=9t$ and $3\mid n$.
  - Explanation: The thirteenth differential is nonzero on the periodicity class and determines which extra scalar and volume families survive.
- **claim-d17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-d17)): On transfers, the length-seventeen chain anchored at a scalar with $w_0=3p$ is $$\label{g:d17-exact} 0\longrightarrow\mathcal T_n \xrightarrow{\quad h\quad}\mathcal W_{n-2} \xrightarrow{\quad h\wedge\quad}\mathcal T_{n-4} \longrightarrow0,$$ with its arrows scaled by $p$ and $p+1$, respectively, up to nonzero units. The degree-two term has zero outgoing.* *For a scalar lift at $w_0=9t$, $3\mid n$, the only possible additional coefficient is $$\label{g:V-form} V(t,i-2n/3)=t\nu+(i-2n/3)\nu'$$ into a one-dimensional restriction kernel in the plane. The ratio of $\nu,\nu'$ is Frobenius-fixed whenever defined.
  - Explanation: On transfers the seventeenth differential forms a short exact chain of transfer modules, with a possible correction into a restriction kernel.
- **claim-to33** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-to33)): After the main length-thirty-three maps, the only potential groups are those in Table <a href="#g:finite-table" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:finite-table">1</a> below, subject to the qualifications following the table.
  - Explanation: After the main contractions only a finite array of one-dimensional groups remains, listed in the table.
- **claim-character-rule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-character)): On every residue-field entry of Table <a href="#g:finite-table" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:finite-table">1</a>, including both subquotients of $\mathsf A$, a normalizing symmetry acts by $$\label{g:character-formula} d_0^{\,2m+3n+\phi},\qquad d_0^2=-1,$$ up to the same orientation-twist factor at fixed $i$, where $$\phi=0\quad\text{on scalar labels and }\mathsf x,\mathsf X, \qquad \phi=2\quad\text{on all other labels}.$$ Consequently a differential of length $R$ can join a source and target only if $$\label{g:character-test} R-\Delta j+3\Delta n+\phi_{\mathrm{target}} -\phi_{\mathrm{source}}\equiv0\pmod4.$$
  - Explanation: A normalizing symmetry acts by an explicit character on every entry of the finite array, giving a congruence rule for the length of any differential.
- **claim-rho-and-e53** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-rho-and-e53)): The residue in Lemma <a href="#g:d13" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="g:d13">41</a> is $\rho=8$. The coefficient $e_{53}$ of the $H$ length-fifty-three differential is nonzero. Moreover, in <a href="#g:V-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:V-form">[g:V-form]</a> one has $\nu\ne0$ and $\nu'=0$.
  - Explanation: The residue parameter is determined and the fifty-third coefficient at the subgroup is nonzero, with the second correction coefficient vanishing.
- **claim-finite-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-finite-comparison)): On page $E_R$, ordinary-to-Tate comparison is surjective in filtration $s\ge0$ and injective in filtration $s\ge R-1$, in every representation twist used here. If a specified ordinary filtration-zero symbol is a Tate cycle through length $R-1$, then it extends as an ordinary class over the $(R-1)$-skeleton.
  - Explanation: Ordinary-to-Tate comparison is surjective and injective in stated filtration ranges, so filtration-zero Tate cycles lift over finite skeleta.
- **claim-omega-nonzero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-omega-norm)): In <a href="#g:omega-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:omega-form">[g:omega-form]</a>, one has $\omega\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The leading coefficient of the fifty-third differential on scalar lifts is nonzero, proved by taking a multiplicative norm and a transfer.
- **claim-d25-nonzero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-d25-nonzero)): Both length-twenty-five arrows in <a href="#g:early-finite-arrows" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:early-finite-arrows">[g:early-finite-arrows]</a> are nonzero on every copy. Thus $\mathsf b,\mathsf v,\mathsf d,\mathsf r$ are all removed at that length.
  - Explanation: Both twenty-fifth-length arrows are nonzero on every copy by a product argument, removing four labels.
- **claim-a-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-a-removal)): The entries $\mathsf a_0,\mathsf a_2$ and their paired $\mathsf R_0,\mathsf R_2$ are removed at length forty-nine. Afterwards $\mathsf a,\mathsf R$ have a common survivor flag only at residue one.* *Let $C_t$ denote the common survivor flag of $\mathsf c_t$ and $\mathsf M_{t+1}$, paired at length thirty-three. Then $$\label{g:Q-C} Q_0=1\quad\Longrightarrow\quad C_1=0.$$
  - Explanation: Two scalar entries and their paired volume entries are removed at length forty-nine, otherwise a required product would be a product of cycles.
- **claim-unit-313** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-unit313)): All translations in $\mathcal L$ are unit cycles through page $E_{313}$. The unit is hit at length $313$ from the $\mathsf f$ translate $$\label{g:last-f} (-5,-36)+\mathcal L.$$ There are no other arrows on the remaining labels at that length, and the next page is zero.
  - Explanation: Translations are unit cycles through the last page, and the unit is hit by a specific translate of a volume class, after which the page is zero.
- **claim-p1-matching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-p1-matching)): One has $C_0=1$. If $C_1=1$, all $\mathsf c_0,\mathsf M_2$ copies pair at length $249$, and all $\mathsf p_1,\mathsf c_1$ copies pair at length $301$. If $C_1=0$, all $\mathsf p_1,\mathsf c_0$ copies pair at length $85$, and neither $\mathsf c_1$ nor $\mathsf M_2$ is present.
  - Explanation: The remaining copies must pair in one of two ways, forced by the absence of other incoming and outgoing arrows.
- **claim-kappa** (theorem, [source](#stmt-g-kappa)): The coefficient in <a href="#g:lattice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="g:lattice">[g:lattice]</a> is $\kappa=-1\in\mathbf F_3$. Equivalently, $$\mathcal L=\{(t,u):t-3u\equiv0\pmod9\}.$$
  - Explanation: A parity argument on the rows of the finite array fixes the lattice coefficient.
- **claim-order-three-322** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cube-order-three)): There is an element $\theta\in\pi_{322}S_{(3)}$ detected by $b_3$ in the mod-three Adams spectral sequence such that $3\theta=0$. In particular, $\theta$ has additive order exactly three.
  - Explanation: There is an actual sphere class in the stem of the third surviving index, detected by the corresponding Kervaire class and killed by three.
- **claim-strong-criterion** (classification, [source](#stmt-cube-strong-criterion)): Let $j\geq0$ and $m=3^{j+1}$. The following conditions are equivalent:* 1. *The detection coset of $b_j$ contains an element of additive order three in $\pi_{4m-2}S$.* 2. *There is a stable map $$\Sigma^{4m-2}M=P^{4m-1}(3)\longrightarrow S$$ whose cone has nonzero reduced power $\mathcal P^m$.* 3. *There is a stable map $a:S^{4m-1}\to M$ for which $$\mathcal P^m:H^0(C_a;\mathbf F_3)\longrightarrow H^{4m}(C_a;\mathbf F_3)$$ is nonzero.* *In condition <span class="upright">(iii)</span>, if $qa=\Sigma\vartheta$, then $3\vartheta=0$ in actual sphere homotopy. The strong criterion identifies $\vartheta$, up to the usual nonzero scalar normalization, as a nonzero class detected by $b_j$.
  - Explanation: An order-three element in the detection coset is equivalent to a stable map from a Moore spectrum whose cone carries a nonzero reduced power, in two equivalent formulations.
- **claim-primary-invariant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cube-primary-invariant)): For a map $u:A\to B$ inducing zero in mod-three homology, the short exact sequence of Steenrod comodules $$0\longrightarrow H_*B\longrightarrow H_*C_u \longrightarrow \Sigma H_*A\longrightarrow0$$ is its ordinary Adams filtration-one invariant. This invariant is natural and additive in $u$. It vanishes for a composite of two maps that induce zero in mod-three homology, and hence for a map of Adams–Novikov filtration at least two.
  - Explanation: The extension class of comodules is a natural additive invariant that vanishes on maps of Adams–Novikov filtration at least two.
- **claim-cubic-filtration** (bound, [source](#stmt-cube-cubic-filtration)): The reduction of the cube satisfies $$ix^3\in F^{10}\pi_{318}M.$$
  - Explanation: The reduction of the cube of the earlier Kervaire element lies in a high Adams–Novikov filtration of the Moore spectrum homotopy.
- **claim-lens-thom** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cube-lens-thom)): The base $\mathcal B$ has cohomological dimension at most two for 3-local local coefficient systems, and $$\widetilde H^*(\mathcal B;A)=0$$ for every constant 3-local abelian group $A$. Moreover, $$\label{cube:thom-identifications} D^\circ(S^1)\simeq\Sigma^4M,\qquad D^\circ(S^n)\simeq\Sigma^NM.$$ These identifications can be chosen so that $$\label{cube:quotient-cube} D^\circ(qa_0)=x^3 1_M: \Sigma^NM\longrightarrow\Sigma^4M.$$
  - Explanation: The truncated base has small cohomological dimension and its Thom spectra on spheres are suspensions of the Moore spectrum compatible with the cube.
- **claim-attachment** (construction, [source](#stmt-cube-attachment)): There is a triangle $$\label{cube:T-triangle} \Sigma^3M\xrightarrow{g}M\xrightarrow{j_T}T \xrightarrow{\pi_T}\Sigma^4M$$ and a map $F:\Sigma^NM\to T$ such that $$\pi_TF=x^3 1_M.$$ Both $j_T$ and the natural map $D^\circ M\to T$ are isomorphisms on bottom mod-three homology.
  - Explanation: A triangle builds the four-cell spectrum receiving the cube map, with comparisons that are isomorphisms on bottom homology.
- **claim-primary-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cube-primary-detection)): The cone of $F:\Sigma^NM\to T$ has a nonzero operation $$\mathcal P^{81}:H^0(C_F;\mathbf F_3)\longrightarrow H^{N+2}(C_F;\mathbf F_3).$$
  - Explanation: The cone of the cube map has a nonzero primary reduced power, shown by a diagonal cone construction and the Euler pairing.
- **claim-endomorphisms** (construction, [source](#stmt-cube-endomorphisms)): There is a unital multiplication $\mu:M\wedge M\to M$, and evaluation on the bottom cell admits a splitting $$\label{cube:end-splitting} \mathcal E=F(M,M)\simeq M\vee\Sigma^{-1}M.$$ Write $\varepsilon:\mathcal E\to M$ for evaluation, $s:M\to\mathcal E$ for its section, and $\tau:\mathcal E\to\Sigma^{-1}M$ for the other projection. The second summand includes by precomposition with $q:M\to\Sigma S$, and $s(i)=1_M$. No associativity of $\mu$ is required.
  - Explanation: The Moore spectrum has a unital multiplication and its endomorphism spectrum splits into two summands.
- **claim-relative-tower** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cube-relative-tower)): Let $\widetilde E_r$ be the spectral sequence of $$\cdots\longrightarrow F_4M\longrightarrow F_3M \longrightarrow F_2M,$$ with absolute filtration indices retained. Its comparison $c_r:\widetilde E_r^{f,t}\to E_r^{f,t}(M)$ has the following properties: $$\begin{array}{c\|c\|c} \text{page}&\text{surjective}&\text{isomorphic}\\ \hline 5&f\geq2&f>2\\ 6=9&f\geq2&f\geq7\\ 10&f\geq2&f\geq11. \end{array}$$ These assertions hold also for maps induced by fixed endomorphisms of $M$, with their appropriate degree shifts.
  - Explanation: The tower starting at filtration two compares with the full tower with stated surjectivity and isomorphism ranges on the relevant pages.
- **claim-correction** (construction, [source](#stmt-cube-correction)): There is a map $F':\Sigma^NM\to F_2T$ whose composite to $T$ has the same quotient under $\pi_T$ as $F$.
  - Explanation: A map into the second filtration of the four-cell spectrum has the same quotient as the cube map, by vanishing of two obstruction components.
- **claim-unit-cubes-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-finite-pairing)): Let $I=\operatorname{fib}(S\longrightarrow\mathrm{BP})$ and $F_qZ=I^{\wedge q}\wedge Z$. For the augmented Amitsur cosimplicial spectrum $$X^n(Z)=\mathrm{BP}^{\wedge(n+1)}\wedge Z$$ there are natural equivalences $$\operatorname{fib}\bigl(Z\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Tot}}_m X^\bullet(Z)\bigr) \simeq F_{m+1}Z,$$ compatible with the maps as $m$ varies. A unital binary multiplication $\mu\colon M\wedge M\longrightarrow M$ induces pairings on its Adams–Novikov spectral sequence with the Leibniz rule on every page. The pairing on $E_2(M)$ is the ordinary graded-commutative Ext product. These statements do not require an associative multiplication on $M$.
  - Explanation: The Amitsur tower is identified with the unit-cube tower and a unital Moore multiplication gives a Leibniz pairing on every page, without associativity.
- **claim-finite-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-finite-main)): Suppose $z\in E_2^{1,108}(M)=D_{1,27}$ is a permanent cycle whose coefficient on $t_1^{27}$ is $1$. Then:* 1. *$z\equiv y\pmod{\mathbf F_3v^{26}a}$ and $B(y)^3=0$ in $D$;* 2. *multiplication by $v$ is zero from $E_6^{10,328}(M)$ to $E_6^{10,332}(M)$. The same holds on $E_9$ and $E_{10}$;* 3. *$E_{10}^{f,322+f}(M)=0$ for every $f\equiv2\pmod4$ with $f\geq14$.
  - Explanation: A permanent cycle with unit leading coefficient has a prescribed form, multiplication by the first generator vanishes on the stated pages, and high filtrations vanish on the tenth page.
- **claim-bounds-valid** (bound, [source](#stmt-finite-bounds-valid)): For every parameter value $u$ one has $$K^-\subseteq\ker A(u)\subseteq K^+, \qquad I^-\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{im}}A(u)\subseteq I^+.$$
  - Explanation: The lower and upper kernel and image subspaces enclose the kernel and image of every matrix in an affine family, with no independence assumption.
- **claim-highweight** (bound, [source](#stmt-finite-highweight)): For $k\geq1$, multiplication by $b^k$ into $D_{f,d}$ is surjective if $$\label{finite:surjective-bound} d<S_k(f):=\frac92f+\frac12-6k,$$ and injective if $$\label{finite:injective-bound} d<I_k(f):=\frac92f-4-6k=S_k(f-1).$$
  - Explanation: Multiplication by powers of the beta class is surjective or injective into high-weight groups under explicit linear inequalities.
- **claim-homology-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-finite-homology-transfer)): Let $q=b^k$ and let $t$ be a target slot.* 1. *If multiplication by $q$ is surjective into $D_t$ and injective into $D_{t+J}$, it is surjective into $H_t$.* 2. *If it is injective into $D_t$ and surjective into $D_{t-J}$, it is injective into $H_t$.
  - Explanation: Surjectivity and injectivity of multiplication on the cochain level pass to the homology of the differential under degree shifts.
- **claim-additive-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-alg-additive-contraction)): If $\iota:B\to G_0$ sends $b$ to $bg_0$, then $$\partial^0H+H\partial^0=1-\iota\varepsilon.$$ The map $H$ preserves the coefficient $v^Q$, the number $\|T\|+w(g)$, and the tuple $T+C(g)$.
  - Explanation: An explicit operator is a contracting homotopy of the additive complex up to the augmentation and preserves several weights.
- **claim-resolution-correct** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-alg-resolution-correct)): Starting with $\partial_1=\partial^0_1$ and augmentation <a href="#alg:augmentation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:augmentation">[alg:augmentation]</a>, the recursion $$\label{alg:deformation} \partial_sg=p- \operatorname{Ant}_{s-2}(\partial_{s-1}p;N), \qquad p=\partial^0_sg,\qquad s\geq2,$$ defines compatible finite truncations of a completed free resolution of the $\mathcal U$-module $B$. Every call to <a href="#alg:antidifferential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:antidifferential">[alg:antidifferential]</a> terminates. Cochains of internal weight $d\leq N$ in this resolution calculate the ordinary normalized-cobar cohomology $D_{s,d}$.
  - Explanation: The recursion with the antidifferential terminates and yields compatible truncations of a free resolution computing cobar cohomology.
- **claim-product-conventions** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-alg-product-conventions)): The rule <a href="#alg:product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:product">[alg:product]</a> calculates signed Yoneda products, with the first factor lifted first. It is independent on cohomology of all choices of lifts and representatives. It agrees with the ordinary Ext product with the corresponding ordering convention. If the cobar comparison instead uses the ordinary opposite ordering of the two factors, graded commutativity changes the product formula by $(-1)^{jf}$ for factors of filtrations $j$ and $f$. This alternative does not alter the use of the affine systems or of their kernel and image bounds.
  - Explanation: The lifting rule computes signed Yoneda products independent of choices; the alternative ordering changes only a sign.
- **claim-square-soundness** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-alg-square-soundness)): Every actual square-zero differential satisfying the imposed Leibniz equations remains in the affine space at every refinement step. The refinement terminates. It makes no assertion that every remaining point is square-zero.
  - Explanation: Linear consequences of the square-zero condition retain every actual differential, and the refinement terminates.
- **claim-enclosures-valid** (bound, [source](#stmt-alg-enclosures-valid)): For every actual matrix $A(u)$ in the affine family, $$K^-\subseteq\ker A(u)\subseteq K^+, \qquad I^-\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{im}}A(u)\subseteq I^+.$$ These inclusions remain valid when parameters shared with other matrices are correlated or satisfy further equations.
  - Explanation: The matrix recipes for kernel and image enclosures are valid for every actual matrix in the affine family.
- **claim-table-verification** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-alg-table-verification)): The recurrences and elimination instructions in this appendix give the low-cohomology ranks, the affine ranks, the sixteen enclosure rows, the sum dimensions, the two cancellation triples, the three injection tests, and the five ordinary product ranks displayed in Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">7</a>. In particular the weight-$60$ system is consistent exactly for $(k,e)=(1,0)$, the stable weight-$92$ affine space has dimension $226$, and $B(y)^3=0$ in $D_{6,81}$.
  - Explanation: The recurrences and elimination instructions reproduce every displayed rank, dimension and cancellation test, by a finite calculation over the field of three elements.

```json
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-coef-tangent",
        "exact_quote": "For $1\\le i\\le5$, the operator $D_g$ sends $P_i$ into the span of $P_0,\\ldots,P_{i-1}$, with a nonzero coefficient of $P_{i-1}$. The operator $D_\\sigma$ lowers the index by at least three, and its coefficient at $P_{i-3}$, for $i\\ge3$, is $a^{3^{i-3}}$, where $$a\\,a^{27}=-1.$$ Moreover, $D_g u/u$ has a nonzero $P_5$-term and $D_\\sigma u/u$ has leading term $a^{27}P_3$. One may choose a weight-one period $u$ and linear coordinates $$y_0=[D_\\sigma u/u],\\qquad y_1=P_4,\\qquad y_2=P_5,\\qquad\n l_i=D_\\sigma y_i$$ such that $$l_0=-P_0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{gr}_{\\mathfrak m}E_*\n   =k[u^{\\pm1},y_0,l_0,y_1,l_1,y_2,l_2],\n \\qquad\n \\sigma y_i=y_i+l_i,\\quad \\sigma l_i=l_i,\\quad \\sigma u=u.$$ The weights of $y_0,y_1,y_2$ are $0,2,8$, respectively.",
        "source_id": "PM-309.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-coef-tangent",
          "exact_quote": "For $1\\le i\\le5$, the operator $D_g$ sends $P_i$ into the span of $P_0,\\ldots,P_{i-1}$, with a nonzero coefficient of $P_{i-1}$. The operator $D_\\sigma$ lowers the index by at least three, and its coefficient at $P_{i-3}$, for $i\\ge3$, is $a^{3^{i-3}}$, where $$a\\,a^{27}=-1.$$ Moreover, $D_g u/u$ has a nonzero $P_5$-term and $D_\\sigma u/u$ has leading term $a^{27}P_3$. One may choose a weight-one period $u$ and linear coordinates $$y_0=[D_\\sigma u/u],\\qquad y_1=P_4,\\qquad y_2=P_5,\\qquad\n l_i=D_\\sigma y_i$$ such that $$l_0=-P_0,\\qquad\n \\operatorname{gr}_{\\mathfrak m}E_*\n   =k[u^{\\pm1},y_0,l_0,y_1,l_1,y_2,l_2],\n \\qquad\n \\sigma y_i=y_i+l_i,\\quad \\sigma l_i=l_i,\\quad \\sigma u=u.$$ The weights of $y_0,y_1,y_2$ are $0,2,8$, respectively.",
          "source_id": "PM-309.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For $1\\le i\\le5$, the operator $D_g$ sends $P_i$ into the span of $P_0,\\ldots,P_{i-1}$, with a nonzero coefficient of $P_{i-1}$.         "anchor": "stmt-coef-graded-tate",
        "exact_quote": "There is an isomorphism of trigraded algebras $$\\label{coef:first-page}\n \\mathcal E_1\n =k[b^{\\pm1},u^{\\pm1},N_0,N_1,N_2]\n       \\otimes\\Lambda(e,z_0,z_1,z_2).$$ Here $e,z_i$ have cohomological degree one and internal degree zero. Their cyclic cocycle values at $\\sigma$ are $1,y_i$, respectively. Their $\\mathfrak m$-filtrations are $0,1$, and $N_i$ has filtration three.         "source_id": "PM-309.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-coef-integral-lift",
        "exact_quote": "After compatible nonzero scalar normalizations, the auxiliary coefficient spectral sequence has $$d_1(u)=uz_0,\\qquad\n d_1(e)=ez_0,\\qquad\n d_1(z_i)=z_i z_0\\ (i=1,2),\\qquad d_1(z_0)=0.$$ Consequently $$\\label{coef:second-page}\n \\mathcal E_2\n =k[b^{\\pm1},u^{\\pm3},N_0,N_1,N_2]\n       \\otimes\\Lambda(eu,z_1u,z_2u,z_0).$$ Its only nonzero differential on these generators is $$d_2(z_0)=c\\,bN_0,\\qquad c\\in k^\\times.$$ There are no further differentials. In particular the leading cohomology class $eu$ is represented by an actual element $x_0\\in E_{-2}$ satisfying $$x_0/u\\equiv1\\pmod{\\mathfrak m},\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}_H(x_0)=0.$$ The element $x_0$ may be chosen of $T$-weight one.",
        "source_id": "PM-309.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-309.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-coef-h-cohomology",
        "exact_quote": "Put $q_i=\\mathop{\\mathrm{N}}_H(x_i)$, $q=q_0$. There are norm parameters $n_1,n_2$ such that, with $K=k[[n_1,n_2]]$, $$\\label{coef:h-tate-ring}\n \\widehat H^*(H;E_*)=\n K[q^{\\pm1},b^{\\pm1}]\\otimes_K\\Lambda_K(V),\n \\qquad |q|=(0,-6),\\quad |b|=(2,0),\\quad |V|=(1,4).$$ The module $V$ is free of rank three, with cyclic-cocycle basis $$a_i=x_i/q_i,\\qquad 0\\le i<3.$$ The group $g$ cyclically permutes this basis semilinearly over $K$. Equivalently, $q_i/q-1$, $i=1,2$, are regular parameters of $K$. For $\\mathfrak n=(n_1,n_2)$, the weights on $\\mathfrak n/\\mathfrak n^2$ are $6,-2$, and $V/\\mathfrak n V$ is a single three-term Jordan block for $g$.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-coef-fixed-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "The eigenvalue condition $$\\sigma x_i=\\zeta x_i$$ defines a framed strict formal $\\mathcal O$-module deformation space of relative height three. Its ungraded base and Lie period are $$D_0=W(k)[\\zeta][[r_1,r_2]],\\qquad\n r_i=x_i/x_0-1\\ (i=1,2),\\qquad w=x_0.$$ The full ungraded $H$-fixed base is equivalently $$\\label{coef:nonreduced-fixed}\n W(k)[[s,r_1,r_2]]/(s^2+3s+3),\\qquad 1+s=\\sigma x_0/x_0.$$ In particular its characteristic-three fibre retains the relation $s^2=0$.*\n\n*Set $\\bar D=D_0/(\\pi)$. There is a $\\mathcal C$-invariant Hasse section $h_1$ in the line $w^{-2}$, given by the first strict coefficient of $\\sigma$. On its zero divisor there is a $\\mathcal C$-invariant Hasse section $h$ in $w^{-8}$, given by the second strict coefficient. The ring $$D'=\\bar D/(h_1)$$ is a formal disc with parameter $hw^8$; here ideals generated by line sections are read after a period trivialization.*\n\n*Evaluation in cyclic cohomology and reduction modulo $\\pi$ identify $$K\\cong\\bar D^{\\,3},\\qquad q\\longmapsto w^3.$$ The evaluated $V$ is the $\\bar D^3$-span of $1,x_1/x_0,x_2/x_0$ in the line $w^{-2}$. There is a $\\mathcal C$-fixed primitive element $\\theta\\in V$, the restriction of the class of $t_1$, which evaluates to $h_1$ up to a nonzero scalar.*\n\n\n*One may choose a $T$-basis $v,\\theta,y$ of $V$ with weights $-2,0,6$, with $v=x_0/q$. Modulo $\\mathfrak n$, $$\\label{coef:jordan}\n g\\theta=\\theta,\\qquad\n gy=y+c_1\\theta,\\qquad\n gv=v+c_2y+c_3\\theta,\\qquad c_1c_2\\ne0.$$ Using evaluation to identify cube sections with their unique preimages, put $$J=h_1^3\\in Kq^{-2},\\qquad F=q^2J,\\qquad\n S_0=K/(F)\\cong(D')^3,\\qquad\n W=(V/K\\theta)\\otimes_K S_0.$$ Then $F$ is a regular parameter of $K$, and there is an injective $\\mathcal C$-equivariant evaluation $$W\\longrightarrow w^{-2}\\quad\\text{over }D'.$$ After trivializing the line, the evaluations of $v,y$ have orders zero and one, respectively.",
        "source_id": "PM-309.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-coef-fixed-geometry",

There is a $\\mathcal C$-invariant Hasse section $h_1$ in the line $w^{-2}$, given by the first strict coefficient of $\\sigma$. On its zero divisor there is a $\\mathcal C$-invariant Hasse section $h$ in $w^{-8}$, given by the second strict coefficient. The ring $$D'=\\bar D/(h_1)$$ is a formal disc with parameter $hw^8$; here ideals generated by line sections are read after a period trivialization.*\n\n*Evaluation in cyclic cohomology and reduction modulo $\\pi$ identify $$K\\cong\\bar D^{\\,3},\\qquad q\\longmapsto w^3.$$ The evaluated $V$ is the $\\bar D^3$-span of $1,x_1/x_0,x_2/x_0$ in the line $w^{-2}$. There is a $\\mathcal C$-fixed primitive element $\\theta\\in V$, the restriction of the class of $t_1$, which evaluates to $h_1$ up to a nonzero scalar.*\n\n\n*One may choose a $T$-basis $v,\\theta,y$ of $V$ with weights $-2,0,6$, with $v=x_0/q$. Modulo $\\mathfrak n$, $$\\label{coef:jordan}\n g\\theta=\\theta,\\qquad\n gy=y+c_1\\theta,\\qquad\n gv=v+c_2y+c_3\\theta,\\qquad c_1c_2\\ne0.$$ Using evaluation to identify cube sections with their unique preimages, put $$J=h_1^3\\in Kq^{-2},\\qquad F=q^2J,\\qquad\n S_0=K/(F)\\cong(D')^3,\\qquad\n W=(V/K\\theta)\\otimes_K S_0.$$ Then $F$ is a regular parameter of $K$, and there is an injective $\\mathcal C$-equivariant evaluation $$W\\longrightarrow w^{-2}\\quad\\text{over }D'.$$ After trivializing the line, the evaluations of $v,y$ have orders zero and one, respectively.",

The ring $$D'=\\bar D/(h_1)$$ is a formal disc with parameter $hw^8$; here ideals generated by line sections are read after a period trivialization.*\n\n*Evaluation in cyclic cohomology and reduction modulo $\\pi$ identify $$K\\cong\\bar D^{\\,3},\\qquad q\\longmapsto w^3.$$ The evaluated $V$ is the $\\bar D^3$-span of $1,x_1/x_0,x_2/x_0$ in the line $w^{-2}$. There is a $\\mathcal C$-fixed primitive element $\\theta\\in V$, the restriction of the class of $t_1$, which evaluates to $h_1$ up to a nonzero scalar.*\n\n\n*One may choose a $T$-basis $v,\\theta,y$ of $V$ with weights $-2,0,6$, with $v=x_0/q$. Modulo $\\mathfrak n$, $$\\label{coef:jordan}\n g\\theta=\\theta,\\qquad\n gy=y+c_1\\theta,\\qquad\n gv=v+c_2y+c_3\\theta,\\qquad c_1c_2\\ne0.$$ Using evaluation to identify cube sections with their unique preimages, put $$J=h_1^3\\in Kq^{-2},\\qquad F=q^2J,\\qquad\n S_0=K/(F)\\cong(D')^3,\\qquad\n W=(V/K\\theta)\\otimes_K S_0.$$ Then $F$ is a regular parameter of $K$, and there is an injective $\\mathcal C$-equivariant evaluation $$W\\longrightarrow w^{-2}\\quad\\text{over }D'.$$ After trivializing the line, the evaluations of $v,y$ have orders zero and one, respectively.",
      "explanation": "The eigenvalue condition defines a strict formal module deformation space whose Hasse sections give a formal disc and a Jordan structure on the exterior module.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-coef-fixed-geometry",
          "exact_quote": "The eigenvalue condition $$\\sigma x_i=\\zeta x_i$$ defines a framed strict formal $\\mathcal O$-module deformation space of relative height three.",
          "source_id": "PM-309.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-h-unit-death",
        "exact_quote": "The only possible differential between $E_{34}(H)$ and $E_{105}(H)$ is $$\\label{h:d53}\n d_{53}(C)=e_{53}A^{-8}C^2U,\\qquad d_{53}(U)=0,\n \\qquad e_{53}\\in k.$$ The class $A^{18}C^{-2}U$ survives to $E_{105}(H)$, and $$\\label{h:d105}\n d_{105}(A^{18}C^{-2}U)\\in k^{\\times}.$$ In particular, $E_{106}(H)=0$.",

      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-h-differentials",
        "exact_quote": "The $H$-Tate calculation has the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The first differential is $$d_5(b^m q^n\\xi)\n        =(m+2n+2j)b^{m+2}q^n\\theta\\wedge\\xi,\n        \\qquad \\xi\\in\\Lambda^jV,$$ up to a single nonzero scalar normalization. Its cohomology has generators $A,B=b^3,\\Theta=b^{-1}\\theta$ and $b^{-2}(V/K\\theta)$ as in <a href=\"#h:e6\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:e6\">[h:e6]</a>, with $\\mu=1$. For a fixed $c\\in k^{\\times}$, the next differential is $$d_9(\\Theta)=cA^{-2}F=cb^4J.$$ Equivalently, on surviving $\\Theta$-containing representatives, $$d_9\\bigl([b^m q^n\\theta\\wedge\\xi]\\bigr)\n         =c[b^{m+5}q^nJ\\xi],$$ where $\\xi$ is a complementary exterior monomial. These raw products are interpreted in the $A,B$ normal form: $A^{-2}$ is a surviving unit, while $b^4$ and $J$ need not survive separately. Its cohomology is <a href=\"#h:e10\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:e10\">[h:e10]</a>.*\n\n2.  *The $d_{17}$ is wedge multiplication by the invariant Hasse vector $h$, with common unit shifts and coefficients $p$ on exterior degree zero and $p-1$ on exterior degree one. Its survivors have supports $$\\begin{array}{c|c}\n      \\text{component}&p\\pmod3\\\\\\hline\n      \\text{scalars}&0\\\\\n      \\ell&2\\\\\n      W/\\ell&1\\\\\n      \\det W&0.\n      \\end{array}$$*\n\n3.  *The nonzero $d_{33}$ contracts $\\ell$ to scalars and $\\det W$ to $W/\\ell$. Under the identifications by $h$ and $h\\wedge(-)$, respectively, these maps are multiplication by $h^3$, up to nonzero scalar units and the prescribed degree shifts. Only scalars at $p\\equiv0$ and quotient classes at $p\\equiv1$ remain, now over $S_0/(Z)=k$.*\n\n4.  *The remaining algebra and differentials are <a href=\"#h:e34\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:e34\">[h:e34]</a>, <a href=\"#h:d53\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:d53\">[h:d53]</a>, and <a href=\"#h:d105\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"h:d105\">[h:d105]</a>. The coefficient $e_{53}$ has not been asserted nonzero in this subgroup calculation.",

        "anchor": "stmt-g-mackey",
        "exact_quote": "Suppressing powers of $b$ and the orientation twist $u'$, the scalar and exterior-degree-three groups at $G$ have a common description $\\widetilde P_n$. Restriction fits into $$0\\longrightarrow \\overline k_n\\longrightarrow\n \\widetilde P_n\\xrightarrow{\\operatorname{res}}P_n^Q\n \\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad\n \\overline k_n=\n \\begin{cases}k,&3\\mid n,\\\\0,&3\\nmid n.\n \\end{cases}$$ Transfer embeds $(P_n)_Q$ in $\\widetilde P_n$, with cokernel $\\overline k_n$. Moreover $$\\label{g:P-tate}\n \\widehat H^d(Q;P_n)=\\overline k_n\n \\qquad(d\\in\\mathbf Z).$$ For $3\\mid n$, the exceptional class is represented by $\\Delta^{n/3}$; its transfer is the nonzero three-multiple in the restriction kernel. There is a class $c_G$ restricting to $a_0a_1a_2$, and multiplication by $c_G$ identifies the scalar and volume descriptions, including their exceptional kernels.*\n\n*In exterior degrees one and two, restriction is injective onto all invariants. These groups are parametrized by transfer of $P_na_0$ and $P_na_0a_1$, respectively.",
        "source_id": "PM-309.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-early-pages",
        "exact_quote": "At fixed $r_0,n$, the $d_5$ complex in increasing exterior degree is $$\\label{g:d5-complex}\n \\widetilde P_n\\xrightarrow{\\ r_0\\operatorname{res}\\ }P_n\n \\xrightarrow{\\ \\pm(r_0+1)D\\ }P_n\n \\xrightarrow{\\ (r_0+2)\\operatorname{tr}\\ }\\widetilde P_n.$$ Consequently the $E_6$ page has the following rows: $$\\label{g:E6}\n\\begin{array}{c|cccc}\n r_0&j=0&j=1&j=2&j=3\\\\\\hline\n 0&\\widetilde P_n&P_n^Q&0&\\overline k_n\\\\\n 2&\\overline k_n&P_n/P_n^Q&DP_n&\\overline k_n\\\\\n 1&\\overline k_n&0&(P_n)_Q&\\widetilde P_n.\n\\end{array}$$ The orientation $u'$ has zero $d_5$ and $d_9$.",
        "source_id": "PM-309.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-g-e10-description",
        "exact_quote": "Put $$S_n=P_n/JP_{n+2},\\qquad W_n=S_n\\otimes\\overline M.$$ The main $H$ groups on $E_{10}$ are $S_n,W_n,S_n$ in exterior degrees $0,1,2$, respectively, at $w_0\\equiv0\\pmod3$. Define transfer modules $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathcal T_n&=\n \\begin{cases}(S_n)_Q,&n\\equiv0,2\\pmod3,\\\\\n NS_n,&n\\equiv1\\pmod3,\n \\end{cases}\\label{g:Tn}\\\\\n \\mathcal W_n&=\n \\begin{cases}(W_n)_Q,&n\\equiv1,2\\pmod3,\\\\\n NW_n,&n\\equiv0\\pmod3.\n \\end{cases}\\label{g:Wn}\n\\end{aligned}$$ At $G$ the main groups are:*\n\n1.  *in degree zero, a scalar group $\\mathcal S_n$ with transfer part $\\mathcal T_n$ and one additional restriction generator when $3\\mid n$;*\n\n2.  *in degree one, the transfer group $\\mathcal W_n$;*\n\n3.  *in degree two, the transfer group $\\mathcal T_n$.*\n\n*On these transfer groups restriction is norm and transfer is the displayed projection. The additional groups are one $k$ each, only when $3\\mid n$: scalar restriction kernels at $w_0\\equiv1,2\\pmod3$, and volume groups at every $w_0$. The volume is of $3c_G$ kernel type when $w_0\\equiv1$, and of reduced $c_G$ type when $w_0\\equiv0,2$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-g-d13",
        "exact_quote": "Put $T_0=b^5c_G$ on $E_{13}$.         "source_id": "PM-309.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-g-character",
        "exact_quote": "On every residue-field entry of Table <a href=\"#g:finite-table\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"g:finite-table\">1</a>, including both subquotients of $\\mathsf A$, a normalizing symmetry acts by $$\\label{g:character-formula}\n d_0^{\\,2m+3n+\\phi},\\qquad d_0^2=-1,$$ up to the same orientation-twist factor at fixed $i$, where $$\\phi=0\\quad\\text{on scalar labels and }\\mathsf x,\\mathsf X,\n \\qquad\n \\phi=2\\quad\\text{on all other labels}.$$ Consequently a differential of length $R$ can join a source and target only if $$\\label{g:character-test}\n R-\\Delta j+3\\Delta n+\\phi_{\\mathrm{target}}\n -\\phi_{\\mathrm{source}}\\equiv0\\pmod4.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-309.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-309.1"

        "exact_quote": "Let $I=\\operatorname{fib}(S\\longrightarrow\\mathrm{BP})$ and $F_qZ=I^{\\wedge q}\\wedge Z$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Tate pages** ([source](#pm-h-14)): We call the resulting spectral sequence the *Tate pages*.
- **cyclic periodicity class b** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Let $b$ denote cyclic cohomological periodicity, with $\|b\|=(2,0)$.
- **strict formal O-module** ([source](#pm-h-19)): A *strict formal $\mathcal O$-module* means a formal group with $\mathcal O$-action whose induced action on its Lie line is the structural scalar action of $\mathcal O$.
- **tangent difference operator** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For an element $e$ acting on coefficients, let $D_e=e-1$.
- **orbit norm N_i** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Set $$N_i=y_i^3-y_i l_i^2.$$
- **digit** ([source](#alg:generators)): A *digit* is a pair $\nu=(i,j)$ with $i\geq1$, $j\geq0$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-comparison** [claim](#stmt-det-filtration-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-filtration-comparison): Compare filtration towers through the unit map to the mod-three Eilenberg–MacLane spectrum and the Amitsur cochain model. -> A detected class has a permanent two-line representative with Thom image a nonzero multiple of b_j.
- **step-two-line** [claim](#stmt-det-two-line-basis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-two-line-basis): Use the Miller–Ravenel–Wilson two-line theorem, then prove independence by connecting homomorphisms and Thom image by an invariant-ideal argument. -> Explicit basis of the two-line with zero Thom image for parameter above one.
- **step-cm-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-det-cm-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-cm-evaluation): Evaluate on a Lubin–Tate formal module with complex multiplication and track the integral Bockstein. -> Nonzero image in group cohomology with integral target.
- **step-two-layer** [claim](#stmt-det-two-layer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-two-layer): Finite two-layer analysis of the cochain tower. -> Nonzero filtration-two symbol implies nonzero fixed point class.
- **step-actual-detector** [claim](#stmt-det-actual-detector) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-actual-detector): Combine the filtration-two lift, the cohomology evaluation and the two-layer lemma. -> Nonzero finite-order image of detected sphere classes.
- **step-euler-bound** [claim](#stmt-det-euler-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-det-euler-bound): Norm of complex cobordism, geometric fixed points, isotropy separation and the finite boundary lemma. -> Finite powers of Euler classes vanish and unit-killing differentials are bounded.
- **step-integral-lift** [claim](#stmt-coef-integral-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-integral-lift): Adic spectral sequence calculation with weight arguments and a complex-multiplication test, then compactness to lift to an exact cycle. -> Trace-zero period in degree minus two.
- **step-coefficient-model** [claim](#stmt-coef-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-model): Triangular tangent calculation and formal inversion. -> Cyclic presentation of the completed coefficient ring.
- **step-fixed-geometry** [claim](#stmt-coef-fixed-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-fixed-geometry): Compute fixed equations, use rigidity of endomorphism lifts and Hasse sections. -> Fixed deformation space, Hasse sections and Jordan structure.
- **step-hom-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-coef-hom-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-coef-hom-vanishing): Determinant line and invariant meromorphic sections exclude equivariant maps. -> Equivariant vanishing and invariant vector rule.
- **step-subgroup-differentials** [claim](#stmt-h-differentials) / [proof](#proof-stmt-h-differentials): Combine classical differentials, degree rules, the no-homomorphism rule and the Euler bound to force successive differentials. -> Full H-Tate differential structure ending with the unit.
- **step-tail-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-g-tail-exclusion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-g-kappa-2): Finite array matching at the larger group using the known survivor to force an outgoing differential on the lattice containing higher detectors. -> No higher Kervaire class survives.
- **step-finite-main** [claim](#stmt-finite-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-table-verification): Affine family of possible differentials, kernel and image enclosures, and high-weight product bounds. -> Vanishing and annihilation statements used for the Moore correction.
- **step-order-three** [claim](#stmt-cube-order-three) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cube-correction-2): Truncated extended cube, filtration-two correction, strong criterion applied to the corrected Moore map. -> A sphere class killed by three detected by the last surviving index.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-right-unit-low-index**: The Hazewinkel recursion for the right unit gives, for the first two indices, eta_R(v_1)=v_1+3t_1 and eta_R(v_2)=v_2+3t_2+5v_1^3t_1+v_1t_1^3 modulo nine; reduced modulo three this is eta_R(v_1)=v_1 and eta_R(v_2)=v_2+v_1t_1^3-v_1^3t_1.
- **inv-right-unit-integrality**: The rational recursion expressions for the right unit are integral at three, so every coefficient can be reduced modulo any positive modulus after exact rational arithmetic.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-division-in-f3**: Run the logarithm and right-unit recursion directly over the field with three elements to save arithmetic. -> The recursion divides by three, so a computation in characteristic three is undefined and loses the correction terms.
- **anti-bockstein-mod-three**: Use the values reduced modulo three of the right unit to form a Bockstein cochain. -> The reduction kills the term obtained after division by three, so the Bockstein class comes out wrong.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The appendix gives an explicit finite recursion (logarithm coefficients, right-unit coefficients) whose output is a polynomial with integer coefficients that can be reduced modulo a chosen modulus; the first two indices are implemented and checked exactly.

```python
# INPUTS: one line on stdin with two integers n and m
# PRECONDITIONS: 0 <= n <= 2 and m >= 2
# OUTPUTS: the polynomial eta_R(v_n) with coefficients reduced modulo m, one term per line as coef e_v1 e_v2 e_t1 e_t2, terms sorted by exponent tuple, zero coefficients omitted
# TIME: a constant number of small polynomial multiplications, bounded for n <= 2
# SPACE: constant, a handful of sparse polynomials in four variables
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic with Fraction, integrality asserted before reduction
# FAILURE: assertion error if a coefficient is not an integer; n above 2 is outside the implemented range
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

ONE = {(0, 0, 0, 0): Fraction(1)}

def add(a, b, s=1):
    r = dict(a)
    for k, c in b.items():
        r[k] = r.get(k, 0) + s * c
    return {k: c for k, c in r.items() if c != 0}

def mul(a, b):
    r = {}
    for ka, ca in a.items():
        for kb, cb in b.items():
            k = tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(ka, kb))
            r[k] = r.get(k, 0) + ca * cb
    return {k: c for k, c in r.items() if c != 0}

def power(a, e):
    r = ONE
    for _ in range(e):
        r = mul(r, a)
    return r

def scale(a, c):
    return {k: v * c for k, v in a.items()}

def gen(pos):
    e = [0, 0, 0, 0]
    e[pos] = 1
    return {tuple(e): Fraction(1)}

def vv(i):
    return ONE if i == 0 else gen(i - 1)

def tt(i):
    return ONE if i == 0 else gen(1 + i)

n, m = map(int, sys.stdin.read().split())
assert 0 <= n <= 2 and m >= 2
l = [ONE]
for j in range(1, n + 1):
    s = {}
    for i in range(j):
        s = add(s, mul(l[i], power(vv(j - i), 3 ** i)))
    l.append(scale(s, Fraction(1, 3)))
rho = []
for j in range(n + 1):
    s = {}
    for i in range(j + 1):
        s = add(s, mul(l[i], power(tt(j - i), 3 ** i)))
    rho.append(s)
R = [ONE]
for j in range(1, n + 1):
    s = scale(rho[j], 3)
    for i in range(1, j):
        s = add(s, mul(rho[i], power(R[j - i], 3 ** i)), -1)
    R.append(s)
out = []
for k in sorted(R[n]):
    c = R[n][k]
    assert c.denominator == 1
    c = int(c) % m
    if c: out.append('%d %d %d %d %d' % ((c,) + k))
print('\n'.join(out))
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a classification of homotopy classes. It supplies no API, library or program interface that software could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Stable homotopy groups of spheres and spectral-sequence differentials have no dynamical, physical or geometric simulation content. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool or workflow is specified, and the finite tables are tied to this one stem. They are not a general-purpose utility. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The appendix gives an explicit finite procedure over the field of three elements: a recursive resolution with an antidifferential correction loop, then row elimination to get ranks, kernels and images. It is a verification procedure for a specific finite calculation, not an algorithm for the classification itself. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is introduced beyond ordinary matrices and graded modules used inside the calculation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite matrix elimination is offline and exact, with no latency, throughput or circuit-level content. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no primitive, hardness assumption or security reduction. The prime three and finite fields appear only as coefficient fields. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, dataset, loss or statistical model. All claims are exact algebraic or topological statements. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The matching of finite array entries is a deduction by parity and degree rules, not an optimisation or scheduling problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, replication, consensus or fault model appears. The group actions are symmetries of coefficient rings. |
| other (algebraic topology reference data) | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | The classification and its unstable consequences can serve as reference facts for researchers and for validating other computations of mod-three stable stems. They are not an executable construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-prime-three-scope** (hypothesis): The classification of surviving standard Kervaire classes is proved only at the prime three for the odd-primary Adams family; it says nothing about the prime-two Kervaire problem or about other odd primes. Blocked use: ['Quoting the surviving index set for the classical prime-two Kervaire invariant one problem.', 'Transferring survival of the indices in the set to odd primes other than three.']
- **limit-coset-existence-only** (formal_scope): The order-three property is an existence statement inside a detection coset; it does not make every representative of the coset have additive order three. Blocked use: ['Assuming every homotopy element detected by a surviving class is killed by three.', 'Using the set of order-three indices as a statement about all coset elements.']
- **limit-finite-pages-only** (formal_scope): The Euler-class bounds and the unit-killing differentials are statements on finite pages of the Euler-localized spectral sequences; no convergence assertion for the infinite localized tower and no periodicity of the abutment is used or proved. Blocked use: ['Reading the unit-killing differential as a convergence theorem for a Tate abutment.', 'Importing integer-graded periodicity of the fixed point homotopy from these finite-page bounds.']
- **limit-conditional-repair-unassumed** (hypothesis): The corollary excluding the higher detectors needs periodicity and torsion-freeness in degree minus two of the fixed point homotopy; those hypotheses are not established and are not assumed in the rest of the paper. Blocked use: ['Citing the corollary as an unconditional exclusion of the detectors with index at least four.', 'Assuming periodicity or torsion-freeness of the fixed point homotopy groups.']
- **limit-full-gap-not-torsion-free** (counterexample_scope): The infinite-order classes in every even degree refute vanishing in degree minus two, but the proposition asserts neither torsion-freeness of a homotopy group nor periodicity. Blocked use: ['Deducing that the degree minus two group is torsion-free from the existence of infinite-order classes.', 'Using the proposition to decide the periodicity question.']
- **limit-tail-exclusion-by-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The exclusion of the classes with index at least four argues by contradiction with the cycle property of a would-be sphere class; it exhibits no explicit differential value for a particular class beyond a forced outgoing differential on a lattice. Blocked use: ['Reading the exclusion as an explicit computation of the differential on each higher detector.', 'Using it to decide the index three class, which lies outside the excluded lattice.']
- **limit-e53-subgroup-stage** (edge_case): At the subgroup stage the coefficient of the fifty-third differential is not asserted to be nonzero; its nonvanishing is obtained only later from the finite array at the larger group. Blocked use: ['Using the subgroup page structure that assumes the fifty-third coefficient is nonzero.', 'Quoting the subgroup calculation alone as determining the surviving exterior exponents.']
- **limit-unstable-multiplication-existential** (counterexample_scope): The multiplication statement in the unstable consequences is existential; no conclusion is drawn about power maps or loop-space structures on the other classifying spaces at the remaining indices. Blocked use: ['Concluding that the third-power map is null-homotopic on the classifying spaces at other indices.', 'Assuming loop-space structures beyond the displayed product decomposition.']
- **limit-hom-vanishing-no-arbitrary-extension** (formal_scope): The injectivity of evaluation used in the equivariant vanishing is proved only after extending and trivializing a line over the cube ring; it is not asserted after arbitrary extension of coefficients in the two-dimensional source. Blocked use: ['Applying the vanishing after an arbitrary extension of coefficients.', 'Treating the evaluation map on the plane module as injective over any base.']
- **limit-affine-family-not-unique** (approximation): The finite affine family of possible fifth differentials is only a set of necessary conditions: the actual differential lies in it, but uniqueness of the differential or any choice among the remaining parameters is not assumed. Blocked use: ['Treating a point of the affine family as the actual differential.', 'Using the kernel and image enclosures as exact kernels and images.']
- **limit-computation-not-proof** (formal_scope): The finite arithmetic checks verify the stated tables but do not replace the resolution and comparison proof or the topological arguments. Blocked use: ['Offering a rerun of the tables as a proof of the vanishing statements.', 'Using the tables outside the stated weights and filtrations.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We solve the Kervaire invariant problem at the prime three for the standard Kervaire classes in the mod-three Adams spectral sequence. These classes survive precisely at indices 0, 2, and 3, and each surviving detection coset contains an element of additive order three. In particular, there is an order-three Kervaire element in stem 322.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The odd-primary Kervaire invariant problem asks which distinguished classes on the Adams two-line represent stable homotopy classes of spheres. Its strong form asks for representatives killed by the prime. These are different questions: permanence determines an associated-graded class, while the order of a representative also depends on an extension in actual homotopy. We solve both questions for the standard family at the prime three.

Write $$E_2^{s,t}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}_{\mathcal A_3}^{s,t}(\mathbf F_3,\mathbf F_3)
 \Longrightarrow (\pi^S_{t-s})^{\wedge}_3,
 \qquad
 b_j\in E_2^{2,\,4\cdot3^{j+1}}\quad(j\geq0),$$ where $\mathcal A_3$ is the mod-three Steenrod algebra and $b_j$ is the standard Kervaire class. The spectral-sequence and family conventions are those of <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Ravenel](#ref-Ravenel), Theorems 2.1.1 and 3.4.4]. Define $$\begin{aligned}
 K&=\{j\geq0:b_j\text{ survives nontrivially to }E_\infty\},\\
 K_3&=\{j\in K:\text{some class detected by }b_j
                      \text{ has additive order }3\}.
