There is a useful exact shortcut for a one-cocycle $\delta_m(q)$. Let $F_q(g)=qg$, extended $\mathcal U$-linearly. Since $v$ is invariant, it is central in $\mathcal U$ modulo three. Thus <a id="alg:delta-lift"></a>$$\label{alg:delta-lift}
 F_{\delta_m(q)}=\frac{F_q\partial-\partial F_q}{v^m}$$ is well defined, and its value is obtained by replacing each term $v^QP_Tg'$ in $\partial g$ by <a id="alg:delta-lift-terms"></a>$$\label{alg:delta-lift-terms}
 \sum_{0<I\leq T}v^Q[t^I]\delta_m(q)\,P_{T-I}g'.$$ Indeed this is precisely <a href="#alg:operator-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:operator-product">[alg:operator-product]</a> for the commutator with $q$, with its $I=0$ term cancelled. Its augmentation in filtration one is the required digit coefficient of $\delta_m(q)$, and $\partial F_{\delta_m(q)}=-F_{\delta_m(q)}\partial$ follows by expanding the commutator and using $\partial^2=0$. The shortcut hence computes the same products as <a href="#alg:lift-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:lift-recursion">[alg:lift-recursion]</a>.

<a id="stmt-alg-product-conventions"></a>
<div id="alg:product-conventions" class="lemma">

**Lemma 72**. *The rule <a href="#alg:product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:product">[alg:product]</a> calculates signed Yoneda products, with the first factor lifted first. It is independent on cohomology of all choices of lifts and representatives. It agrees with the ordinary Ext product with the corresponding ordering convention. If the cobar comparison instead uses the ordinary opposite ordering of the two factors, graded commutativity changes the product formula by $(-1)^{jf}$ for factors of filtrations $j$ and $f$. This alternative does not alter the use of the affine systems or of their kernel and image bounds.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-alg-product-conventions"><summary>Proof of stmt-alg-product-conventions</summary>

*Proof.* The difference of two lifts with the same augmentation is null homotopic: in each successive degree its uncancelled difference is a cycle, which is lifted by <a href="#alg:antidifferential" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:antidifferential">[alg:antidifferential]</a>. The same argument applied to a coboundary representative proves independence of that representative. Composition of such lifts is the defining Yoneda composition.

One may also compare on the relative bar. An unsigned lift applies the cocycle to the last block of entries and retains the first block; move the resulting coefficient left by the balancing relation and discard scalar entries at normalized positions. The interior terms cancel in its chain identity, and the terms crossing the division between blocks cancel by the cocycle equation. Multiplying its component in degree $s$ by $(-1)^{js}$ gives <a href="#alg:signed-lift-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:signed-lift-identity">[alg:signed-lift-identity]</a>. Evaluation by the other cocycle is the bar, and hence cobar, cup product, with the stated ordering sign.

To check the final assertion explicitly, suppose an odd-filtration differential $D$ is a left derivation for a product juxtaposed as $xy$, and change to $x\star y=(-1)^{fg}xy$, where $x,y$ have filtrations $f,g$. Then $$D(x\star y)=(-1)^gD(x)\star y+x\star D(y).$$ The row rescaling $L|_{D_{f,*}}=(-1)^fD$ therefore obeys the left Leibniz rule for $\star$, and $L^2=-D^2=0$. Row rescaling preserves every kernel, image, and line of possible cycles. A further common unit puts $L(c')=ab^3$ as in Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">7</a>. The permitted unit in the $d_9$ formula absorbs the analogous convention change there. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-56"></a>
#### Elimination and the literal affine systems

We describe the elimination convention so the recipe specifies finite scalar operations throughout. Choose any fixed total order of the monomials and generators extending their weights; for example order generators by $(w(g),C(g),(e_\nu))$ and then coefficient multi-indices lexicographically. Order slots by $(d,f)$ and matrix entries by row then column. Given a sequence of row vectors, maintain a dictionary of rows with distinct first nonzero positions. For a row $z$, let $i$ be its first nonzero position and $c=z_i$. If a stored row $p_i$ has pivot one at $i$, replace $z$ by $z-cp_i$ and repeat at the new first nonzero position until the row is zero or has an unused pivot. Discard a zero row. At an unused pivot, store $c z$ as $p_i$ and continue with the next input row. The normalization uses $c^{-1}=c$ for $c=1,2$ in $\mathbf F_3$. The number of stored rows is the rank. Recording the same operations on an identity matrix gives witnesses for dependencies or for solutions.

To solve a homogeneous system, assign independent parameters to the nonpivot variables and substitute the pivot equations in reverse order. For an affine system append a last constant column and put its variable equal to one. A pivot in that last position is an inconsistency certificate; otherwise the same substitution gives a constant solution plus a basis of homogeneous solutions. Kernel, image, intersection, and quotient computations below use only this procedure. The ranks and intrinsic subspaces are independent of the chosen orders and Ext representatives.

<a id="alg:leibniz-constants"></a>
For $n=60$ or $92$, let <a id="alg:finite-grid"></a>$$\label{alg:finite-grid}
 \mathcal G_n=\{(f,d):0\leq d\leq n,\quad 0\leq f\leq32,
                                      \quad2d-3f\geq0\}.$$ For every pair $T,T+J$ in this grid, $J=(5,1)$, introduce all scalar entries of a matrix $L_T:D_T\to D_{T+J}$ as independent unknowns. Slots of dimension zero contribute no entries. Put $L_T=0$ at filtration zero. For each $(k,e)\in\mathbf F_3^2$ use the factors $$x=a,v,h,b,c',r,s',y+e t_7'.$$ For each complete square $T,T+J,T+x,T+x+J$ in the grid, set <a id="alg:leibniz-equations"></a>$$\label{alg:leibniz-equations}
 L_{T+x}\operatorname{mult}(x,T)
 -(-1)^{f_x}\operatorname{mult}(x,T+J)L_T=Z_{x,T},$$ where $$\label{alg:leibniz-constants}
 Z_{x,T}=
 \begin{cases}
 \operatorname{mult}(a,T+3b)\operatorname{mult}(b^3,T),&x=c',\\
 k\operatorname{mult}(v,T+3b)\operatorname{mult}(b^3,T),&x=r,\\
 0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}$$ For $s'$ and $y+e t_7'$ omit the entire equation if either required factor multiplication has output weight greater than $91$. Include an equation only when every required matrix is available, including intermediate matrices in the displayed successive products. In particular a target lying outside the grid is not silently replaced by a known zero space. This is the literal finite system used in Table [finite:affine-ranks] [finite:affine-ranks](#finite:affine-ranks).

<a id="alg:scalar-leibniz"></a>
Every matrix equation here is a specified list of scalar affine equations. More explicitly its $(\alpha,\beta)$ entry is $$\label{alg:scalar-leibniz}
 \sum_i (L_{T+x})_{\alpha i}
              \operatorname{mult}(x,T)_{i\beta}
 -(-1)^{f_x}\sum_j
              \operatorname{mult}(x,T+J)_{\alpha j}
              (L_T)_{j\beta}-(Z_{x,T})_{\alpha\beta}=0.$$ The named products and coefficients in this formula were fully specified above; in particular the unknown differential is never used to supply an Ext product.

<a id="alg:square-polynomials"></a>
For $n=60$, solve these systems for all nine $(k,e)$. For $n=92$, use $(k,e)=(1,0)$ and refine its affine solution space as follows. Express all entries as affine linear polynomials in its free parameters $u_1,\ldots,u_a$. For every complete triple $T,T+J,T+2J$ expand every entry of $$\label{alg:square-polynomials}
 L_{T+J}(u)L_T(u)$$ as a polynomial of degree at most two. Collect the commutative monomials $u_i u_j$ with $i\leq j$, then $u_i$, then the constant monomial. Eliminate with all quadratic columns placed first. The rows remaining with no quadratic term are affine linear equations; adjoin them to the system, reparameterize, and repeat until no new restriction is obtained.

<a id="stmt-alg-square-soundness"></a>
<div id="alg:square-soundness" class="lemma">

**Lemma 73**. *Every actual square-zero differential satisfying the imposed Leibniz equations remains in the affine space at every refinement step. The refinement terminates. It makes no assertion that every remaining point is square-zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-alg-square-soundness"><summary>Proof of stmt-alg-square-soundness</summary>

*Proof.* Each row produced by elimination is an $\mathbf F_3$-linear combination of the entries of <a href="#alg:square-polynomials" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:square-polynomials">[alg:square-polynomials]</a>. It therefore vanishes at each actual square-zero differential. Rows with no quadratic term are valid affine restrictions on the present parameter space. Substitution of an affine parametrization preserves all such points. Every nontrivial refinement decreases the finite affine dimension, which proves termination. Retaining only the linear consequences uses no claim about all polynomial consequences or about the full set of $\mathbf F_3$-points of the quadratic system. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-57"></a>
#### Universal subspaces and the final rank tests

<a id="alg:kernel-enclosures"></a>
<a id="alg:image-enclosures"></a>
For an outgoing or incoming matrix obtained by projection from the final affine space, write its parametrization as $A(u)=A_0+\sum_{i=1}^a u_iA_i$. Zero or redundant parameter matrices may be discarded. For a column matrix $X$, let $\langle X\rangle$ denote its column span and let $X^\perp$ denote a row matrix whose kernel is $\langle X\rangle$. Such a matrix is obtained by computing $\ker X^{\mathsf t}$ and transposing a basis. The following formulas are explicit matrix versions of the enclosures in Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">7</a>: $$\begin{aligned}
 K^-&=\ker\begin{bmatrix}A_0\\A_1\\\vdots\\A_a\end{bmatrix},
 &K^+&=\ker\bigl([A_1|\cdots|A_a]^\perp A_0\bigr),
 \label{alg:kernel-enclosures}\\
 I^-&=\left\langle A_0\ker\begin{bmatrix}A_1\\\vdots\\A_a\end{bmatrix}
                              \right\rangle,
 &I^+&=\langle[A_0|A_1|\cdots|A_a]\rangle.
 \label{alg:image-enclosures}
\end{aligned}$$ When a space appears as a matrix operand, take any column basis of it. An empty stack of constraints has the full space as its kernel. Negative-filtration groups are zero. At other unavailable incoming or outgoing pairs use the zero space for the lower bound and the entire relevant space for the upper bound.

<a id="stmt-alg-enclosures-valid"></a>
<div id="alg:enclosures-valid" class="lemma">

**Lemma 74**. *For every actual matrix $A(u)$ in the affine family, $$K^-\subseteq\ker A(u)\subseteq K^+,
 \qquad I^-\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{im}}A(u)\subseteq I^+.$$ These inclusions remain valid when parameters shared with other matrices are correlated or satisfy further equations.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-alg-enclosures-valid"><summary>Proof of stmt-alg-enclosures-valid</summary>

*Proof.* Every $A_i$ kills $K^-$. If $A(u)x=0$, then $A_0x=-\sum_{i>0}u_iA_ix$ lies in $\sum_{i>0}\mathop{\mathrm{im}}A_i$, which is the condition defining $K^+$. On the common kernel of the $A_i$ with $i>0$, the map $A(u)$ equals $A_0$, proving the lower image inclusion. The upper image inclusion follows by summing the images of its summands. Each argument applies to every single parameter value, so restricting the allowed parameter values cannot invalidate it. ◻

</details>

<a id="alg:allowed-space"></a>
<a id="alg:cancellation-preimage"></a>
<a id="alg:cancellation-span"></a>
<a id="alg:injection-test"></a>
Use outgoing matrices at a slot $q$ for $K_q^\pm$ and incoming matrices for $I_q^\pm$. Here are matrix recipes for every further test; they also fix precisely which preimages and intersections are meant. For the two sum dimensions in Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">7</a>, compute the ranks of $$[\operatorname{mult}(v,(10,82))K^+_{10,82}\mid I^-_{10,83}],
 \qquad[K^+_{22,86}\mid I^-_{22,86}].$$ For the cancellation test with $m,h_0,\ell,u,p,z$ as specified there, form $$\begin{aligned}
 W_p&=[I^-_p\mid\operatorname{mult}(b^\ell,z)K^-_z],\label{alg:allowed-space}\\
 A&=K^-_u\ker\bigl(W_p^\perp
                         \operatorname{mult}(P,u)K^-_u\bigr),
                         \label{alg:cancellation-preimage}\\
 F&=[I^-_m\mid\operatorname{mult}(b^{h_0},u)A].
                         \label{alg:cancellation-span}
\end{aligned}$$ Take column spans in the second and third lines when computing dimensions. The tested triple is $\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}A,\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}F,\mathop{\mathrm{rank}}[F\mid K^+_m]$. For an injection test at $q=(f,d)$ with exponent $n$, compute $$\label{alg:injection-test}
 \mathop{\mathrm{rank}}\left[I^-_q\ \middle|\quad
 K^+_q\ker\left((I^+_{q+nb})^\perp
                    \operatorname{mult}(b^n,q)K^+_q\right)\right].$$ Together with $\dim I^-_q$, this is exactly the pair recorded in Table [finite:injection-data] [finite:injection-data](#finite:injection-data). One can avoid annihilator matrices altogether: the preimage of $\langle H\rangle$ under a matrix $M$ is the projection to the source of $\ker[M\mid-H]$. This gives an equivalent elimination recipe for all the preimages above.

<a id="alg:second-factorization"></a>
There is a shorter calculation for the second cancellation triple. Here $$u=(12,76),\quad p=(15,92),\quad z=(9,83),\quad
 W_p=I^-_{15,92}+b^3K^-_{9,83}.$$ Compute the image of $a:D_{14,91}\to D_{15,92}$. The ranks of $W_p$, that image, and their sum are all three. Hence $$\label{alg:second-factorization}
 \mathop{\mathrm{im}}(a:D_{14,91}\to D_{15,92})\subseteq W_p.$$ Since $P=aB(t_4')$, every $Px$ in this slot lies in this image. Equation <a href="#alg:cancellation-preimage" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:cancellation-preimage">[alg:cancellation-preimage]</a> therefore has $A=K^-_{12,76}$, of dimension six. Only the $a$ matrix and the $b^3$ matrix are required for this containment calculation. The first cancellation triple uses the actual $P$ matrix from $(4,69)$ to $(7,85)$.

<a id="pm-h-58"></a>
#### Arithmetic checkpoints and reproducibility

<a id="stmt-alg-table-verification"></a>
<div id="alg:table-verification" class="proposition">

**Proposition 75**. *The recurrences and elimination instructions in this appendix give the low-cohomology ranks, the affine ranks, the sixteen enclosure rows, the sum dimensions, the two cancellation triples, the three injection tests, and the five ordinary product ranks displayed in Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">7</a>. In particular the weight-$60$ system is consistent exactly for $(k,e)=(1,0)$, the stable weight-$92$ affine space has dimension $226$, and $B(y)^3=0$ in $D_{6,81}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-alg-table-verification"><summary>Proof of stmt-alg-table-verification</summary>

*Finite calculation.* The following intermediate outputs specify checks on the elimination at each stage, in addition to the final tables. They can be reconstructed without any external list of Ext groups.

First enumerate <a href="#alg:generators" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:generators">[alg:generators]</a> and <a href="#alg:cochain-basis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:cochain-basis">[alg:cochain-basis]</a>, construct <a href="#alg:deformation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:deformation">[alg:deformation]</a>, and form <a href="#alg:cochain-matrix" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:cochain-matrix">[alg:cochain-matrix]</a>. For $(f,d)=(1,27),(6,10),(6,81)$ the dimensions of the three successive cochain terms are respectively $$(12,49,94),\qquad(3,2,1),\qquad(9301,11419,12864).$$ Reducing the incoming and outgoing columns gives ranks $$(11,35),\qquad(1,0),\qquad(5256,6156).$$ For the first slot append, in order, the cochains $v^{26}a,y,t_7'$; the rank becomes $14$. For the second append $vb^3$; the rank becomes $2$. For the third construct $B(y)$ by <a href="#alg:bockstein" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:bockstein">[alg:bockstein]</a>, apply <a href="#alg:get-two" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:get-two">[alg:get-two]</a>, and form its cube using <a href="#alg:lift-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:lift-recursion">[alg:lift-recursion]</a>–<a href="#alg:product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:product">[alg:product]</a>. Appending this cubic leaves the incoming rank $5256$. Keeping witnesses in column elimination expresses the cubic as a boundary. In these coordinates $B(y)$ has eight coefficient terms on six generators, the cubic has $76$ terms, and the resulting preimage can be taken to have $2642$ terms. Substitution in <a href="#alg:cochain-matrix" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:cochain-matrix">[alg:cochain-matrix]</a> verifies its boundary directly. This proves the algebraic zero in the statement.

For weight $60$, the grid contains $1213$ slots and its complete pairs have $2779$ scalar unknowns. Expanding the zero and Leibniz equations gives $15852$ scalar equations. For each of the nine $(k,e)$ choices, the coefficient rank is $2667$. The augmented rank is $2667$ for $(1,0)$ and $2668$ for each of the other eight choices. These values are obtained from <a href="#alg:scalar-leibniz" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:scalar-leibniz">[alg:scalar-leibniz]</a>, including its constant column; the pivot at the constant position is an explicit linear combination giving $1=0$ in each inconsistent case.

At weight $92$ there are $2269$ slots, of total Ext dimension $6341$, and $1820$ differential pairs with $23384$ entries. The Leibniz assembly uses $15453$ factor multiplication matrices, including successive factors in <a href="#alg:leibniz-constants" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:leibniz-constants">[alg:leibniz-constants]</a>. There are $137471$ scalar zero and Leibniz equations, $110060$ of them having nonzero augmented row. Coefficient and augmented ranks are both $23119$. Thus the initial affine dimension is $265$. The successive square eliminations have the following checkpoints: $$\begin{array}{r|r|r|r}
 \text{parameters}&\text{scalar square equations}&
       \text{polynomial row rank}&\text{new affine restrictions}\\\hline
 265&16412&72&38\\
 227&16412&20&1\\
 226&16412&19&0
\end{array}$$ In each row quadratic monomials precede linear ones, as specified after <a href="#alg:square-polynomials" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:square-polynomials">[alg:square-polynomials]</a>; after the final refinement the affine rank is $23158$. Apply <a href="#alg:kernel-enclosures" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:kernel-enclosures">[alg:kernel-enclosures]</a> and <a href="#alg:image-enclosures" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:image-enclosures">[alg:image-enclosures]</a> to its projected matrices. They give the sixteen rows of Table [finite:enclosures] [finite:enclosures](#finite:enclosures). The two additional stacked ranks are $1$ and $2$. Equations <a href="#alg:allowed-space" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:allowed-space">[alg:allowed-space]</a>–<a href="#alg:cancellation-span" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:cancellation-span">[alg:cancellation-span]</a>, using <a href="#alg:second-factorization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:second-factorization">[alg:second-factorization]</a> in the second case, give $(4,5,5)$ and $(6,5,5)$. Equation <a href="#alg:injection-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="alg:injection-test">[alg:injection-test]</a> gives the pairs $(3,3),(2,2),(4,4)$.

Finally compute the five iterated $b$ maps of Table [finite:product-ranks] [finite:product-ranks](#finite:product-ranks) with cutoff $93$. As a check independent of quotient-coordinate extraction, append the resulting product cochains to the incoming coboundary columns in their target slots. In the order of that table, incoming ranks and ranks after appending are $$(4491,4499),\quad(7540,7546),\quad(7589,7595),\quad
 (1868,1872),\quad(1000,1004).$$ Their differences are the product ranks $8,6,6,4,4$. All these operations have been reduced to the polynomial recursions, the terminating lifting recursion, and the explicitly stated elimination rules. The equalities used to infer the spectral-sequence conclusions are therefore finite algebra calculations; their use is proved in Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">7</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-2"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 76* (Distinct forms of computational checking). Reconstructing the larger tables by another implementation of this same recurrence checks its arithmetic, while sharing the mathematical resolution construction. There are also ordinary normalized-cobar calculations which do not use the smaller resolution. For example in weight $27$ their dimensions in filtrations zero and one are $12$ and $470$, and their incoming and outgoing ranks at filtration one are $11$ and $456$. They give the same three-dimensional group with the representatives $v^{26}a,y,t_7'$. At $(6,10)$ their middle dimension is $253$, the incoming and outgoing ranks are $162$ and $90$, and appending $vb^3$ increases the incoming rank by one. These different complex sizes distinguish the ordinary-cobar checks from a replay of the smaller-resolution calculation. Verification of the cubic boundary, substitution into the affine equations, and dense elimination for the final subspaces provide further arithmetic checks. None replaces the resolution and comparison proof above or the topological arguments of Section <a href="#sec:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:finite">7</a>.

</div>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The final constraint in row $6$. The known survivor $b_{2}$ forces the $d_{93}$ pairing, so its source $y$ cannot also hit $p_{0}$ at length $309$. The possible $x$ incoming at length $273$ lies in row $3$. Thus $p_{0}$ must support one of the two indicated outgoing differentials; the argument does not choose between them. The diagram records logical constraints, not chronological order: length $85$ precedes length $93$.",
      "figure_id": "g:forcing-figure",
      "pdf_page": 57,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "g:forcing-figure",
          "exact_quote": "The final constraint in row $6$.  The known survivor $b_2$\nforces the $d_{93}$ pairing, so its source $\\mathsf y$ cannot also\nhit $\\mathsf p_0$ at length $309$.  The possible $\\mathsf x$ incoming\nat length $273$ lies in row $3$.  Thus $\\mat",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  >=Stealth,\n  every node/.style={font=\\small},\n  entry/.style={inner sep=5pt},\n  arrowlabel/.style={fill=white,inner sep=2pt,align=center}\n]\n  \\draw[rounded corners,black!35] (-0.55,-1.95) rectangle (10.25,1.25);\n  \\node[anchor=west,fill=white,inner sep=3pt] at (-0.3,1.25)\n    {Row $6$};\n  \\node[entry] (y) at (0.4,0) {$\\mathsf y$};\n  \\node[entry,align=center] (p2) at (4.2,0.65)\n    {$\\mathsf p_2$\\\\[-1pt]\\scriptsize detector for $b_2$};\n  \\node[entry,align=center] (p0) at (4.2,-0.85)\n    {$\\mathsf p_0$\\\\[-1pt]\\scriptsize detectors for $b_{\\jmath}$, $\\jmath\\ge4$};\n  \\node[draw,rounded corners,align=center,inner sep=6pt] (out) at (8.8,-0.85)\n    {one of\\\\[2pt]\n     $d_{85}:\\ \\mathsf c_2$\\\\[-1pt]\n     $d_{157}:\\ \\mathsf a_1$};\n  \\draw[->,thick] (y) -- node[arrowlabel,above,sloped]\n    {$d_{93}$ forced} (p2);\n  \\draw[->,dashed] (y) -- (p0);\n  \\node[arrowlabel] at (1.5,-1.25) {$d_{309}$ unavailable};\n  \\draw[->,thick] (p0) -- node[arrowlabel,above]\n    {outgoing forced} (out);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A rectangular diagram labeled \"Row 6\" in the top-left corner. On the left is a node labeled $y$. From $y$, two arrows point towards the right: a solid upper arrow labeled \"$d_{93}\\text{ forced}$\" pointing to a target labeled \"$p_2$\" with sublabel \"detector for $b_2$\"; and a dashed lower arrow labeled \"$d_{309}\\text{ unavailable}$\" pointing to a target labeled \"$p_0$\" with sublabel \"detectors for $b_\\jmath, \\jmath \\ge 4$\". From the $p_0$ target, a solid horizontal arrow labeled \"outgoing forced\" points right to a boxed set of choices labeled \"one of\", containing \"$d_{85} : c_2$\" and \"$d_{157} : a_1$\"."
    }
  ],
